**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

दिवे—ऊडिदं पदादि-(पा. सू. ६-१-१७१) (*dive—ūḍidaṃ padādi-(pā. sū. 6-1-171)*) (the sūtra as supplied, "Pā. Sū." with its number): the dative ending takes the acute. अवोचम्—ब्रूञ् (*avocam—brūñ*) (root अवोचम्—ब्रूञ् (*avocam—brūñ*) "to speak clearly"): by छंदसि लुज् लज् लिटः (*chaṃdasi luj laj liṭaḥ*) the *luṅ*, *laṅ* and *liṭ* in the Veda; the *laṅ* in the sense of the present; first person singular; the verb-*nighāta* accent.

### Printed p. 97

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 3.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 97 (as supplied).*

अभिश्रावाय—श्रु (*abhiśrāvāya—śru*) (root अभिश्रावाय—श्रु (*abhiśrāvāya—śru*) "to hear"): the suffix *ac* as in *pacādi*; by सार्वधातु- कार्धधातुकयोः (*sārvadhātu- kārdhadhātukayoḥ*) guṇa of *u* and the substitute *ava*; by श्रव एव श्रावः प्रज्ञादिभ्यश्च (पा सू. ५-४-३८) (*śrava eva śrāvaḥ prajñādibhyaśca (pā sū. 5-4-38)*) (Pā. Sū. 5-4-38) the suffix *aṇ* in the own sense (*śrava eva śrāvaḥ*); by यस्यॆति च (*yasyèti ca*) the loss of *a*; by अतउपधायाः (पा. सू. ७-२-११६) (*ataupadhāyāḥ (pā. sū. 7-2-116)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-116) vṛddhi of the penultimate; by the suffix accent the last syllable is acute; dative singular. सुमेधाः—शोभनं (*sumedhāḥ—śobhanaṃ*) ("one whose understanding is good"): by नञ् सुभ्याम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१७२) (*nañ subhyām (pā. sū. 6-2-172)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-172) the last syllable of the later member is acute. पाताम्—पा (*pātām—pā*) (root पाताम्—पा (*pātām—pā*) "to protect"): imperative, second person dual; because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta*; the suffix accent, last syllable acute. अवद्यात्—पद (*avadyāt—pada*) (root अवद्यात्—पद (*avadyāt—pada*) "to speak"): when *nañ* is the upapada, by अवद्य पण्य वर्यागर्ह्य — (पा सू ३-१-१०१) (*avadya paṇya varyāgarhya — (pā sū 3-1-101)*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-101) the suffix *yat* in the sense of blame, irregularly formed.

> इ॒दं द्या॑वापृथिवी स॒त्यम॑स्तु॒ पित॒र्मात॒र्यदि॒होप॑ब्रु॒वे वां॑ ।

### Printed p. 98

*Running head: [98 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185] (the print has "ಸಾಯಣಭಾವ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ" as supplied).*

> मातः पृथिवि नां युवां प्रतीहास्मिन्यज्ञे यत्सोत्रमुपब्रुवे उपेत्य ब्रवीमि
> तत्सत्यमस्तु । देवानां स्तोत्थणामस्माकमवमे । अंतिकनामैतत् । नित्यसंनिहिते
> युवामवोभिस्तर्पणैर्युक्ते भूतं । भवतं । अवशिष्टं गतं ॥

*Gist:* May this praise be true and fruitful; O father Heaven and mother Earth, be near us with your protections, so that we gain food, strength and long life.

*(The bhāṣya has "ನಾಂ ಯುವಾಂ" where "ವಾಂ" would be expected; kept as supplied, नां युवां (*nāṃ yuvāṃ*) not corrected as the picture covers only the Saṃhitā and Pada.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पितः (*pitaḥ*) — father by nature. मातः (*mātaḥ*) — mother by nature. द्यावापृथिवी (*dyāvāpṛthivī*) — O Heaven and Earth. वां (*vāṃ*) — (addressed) to you both. इह (*iha*) — in this sacrifice. यत् (*yat*) — which praise. उप- ब्रुवे (*upa- bruve*) — (I) approach and recite. इदं (*idaṃ*) — that praise of mine. सत्यं (*satyaṃ*) — may it be. अस्तु (*astu*) — true, giving fruit. देवानां (*devānāṃ*) — for us who praise. अवमे (*avame*) — you two who are near. अवोभिः (*avobhiḥ*) — with acts of protection. भूतं (*bhūtaṃ*) — be. इषं (*iṣaṃ*) — food. वृजनं (*vṛjanaṃ*) — strength. जीरदानुं (*jīradānuṃ*) — long life. विद्याम (*vidyāma*) — may we obtain.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Heaven and Earth, who are mother and father of the whole world: may the praise we recite to you both in this sacrifice give a true fruit. Remaining near us who praise, may you both be joined with acts of protection. By your favour may we obtain food, strength and long life.

**Printed English translation**

May this (hymn), Heaven and Earth, be successful; (the hymn) which, Father and Mother, I repeat to you both on this occasion; be ever with your protection in the proximity of those who praise you, that we may obtain food, strength, and long life.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

In this ṛk the ṛṣi prays to the gods that all the acts of protection and the like described in the praise he has recited may come true, that the presence of the gods may be given, and that food, strength and long life may come; and so he closes the sūkta.

पितः मातः (*pitaḥ mātaḥ*) — as in the previous ṛk, in this ṛk also he prays to Heaven and Earth, with devotion, by the respectful and natural words पितः मातः (*pitaḥ mātaḥ*) (parents).

### Printed p. 99

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 99 (as supplied, "ವ ೪"). The window begins in the Special Topics of Ṛk 11 of Sūkta 185, continuing from p. 98; these first lines (from "ಯತ್ ಇಹ ಉಪಬ್ರುವೇ") are new text, not yet translated.*

यत् इह उपब्रुवे वां इदं (*yat iha upabruve vāṃ idaṃ*) ("May this, which I speak here to you both, be true") — "O gods, may this praise which I, approaching you, recite in your honour bring a true fruit": thus he has plainly stated the devotion in his mind and his desire.

देवानां—ई (*devānāṃ—ī*) — for this word Sāyaṇa has taken the sense स्तोतृणामस्माकं-स्तोत्रमाडुत्तिरुव (*stotṛṇāmasmākaṃ-stotramāḍuttiruva*) ("of us who are praising, the sacrificers and the rest").

अवमे-तळित्, (*avame-taḷit,*) — among the eleven words for "near", such as अवमे-तळित्, (*avame-taḷit,*) and असात्—मॊदलाद (*asāt—mòdalāda*), the word अवमे (*avame*) is read, so this word means "near" ((नि. ३-९) (*(ni. 3-9)*)).

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

द्यावापृथिवी—द्यौश्च (*dyāvāpṛthivī—dyauśca*) ("Heaven and Earth"): by दिवोद्यावा (*divodyāvā*) the substitute *dyāvā*. When a dual ending in the vocative follows, by सुपां सुलुक्—(पा सू ७-१-२९) (*supāṃ suluk—(pā sū 7-1-29)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-29) lengthening to the preceding class vowel; by आ मंत्रितस्य च (पा. सू ८-१-१९) (*ā maṃtritasya ca (pā. sū 8-1-19)*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-19) the *nighāta* accent arises. सत्यम्—सत्सु (*satyam—satsu*) ("good among the good"): by सत्यादशपथे (पा सू- ५-४-६६) (*satyādaśapathe (pā sū- 5-4-66)*) (Pā. Sū. 5-4-66) irregularly formed with the last syllable acute. उपब्रुवे—ब्रूञ् (*upabruve—brūñ*) (root उपब्रुवे—ब्रूञ् (*upabruve—brūñ*) "to speak clearly"): imperative, first person singular; by यद्वृत्तानित्यम् (पा. सू. ८-१-६६) (*yadvṛttānityam (pā. sū. 8-1-66)*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-66) the prohibition of the *nighāta* accent arises, so by the suffix accent the last syllable is acute; by तिजिचोदात्तवति (पा. सू. ८-१-७१) (*tijicodāttavati (pā. sū. 8-1-71)*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-71) the preverb (*upa*) is wholly unaccented. भूतम्—भू (*bhūtam—bhū*) (root भूतम्—भू (*bhūtam—bhū*) "to be"): imperative, second person dual, the substitute *tam*; by बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) the loss of the thematic suffix; by भूसुवोस्तिजि (पा. सू. ७-३-८८) (*bhūsuvostiji (pā. sū. 7-3-88)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-3-88) the prohibition of guṇa; because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta*; by the suffix accent the last syllable is acute.

**Sūkta 186: Sāyaṇa's introduction** (Sanskrit, transcribed)

नूर ऎंभत्तारनॆय सूक्तवु. (*nūra èṃbhattāranèya sūktavu.*)

> आ न इळाभिरिति सप्तमं सूक्तमेकादशर्चमागस्त्यं त्रैष्टुभं वैश्वदेवं ।
> आ नो वैश्वदेवमित्यनुक्रांतं ॥ विनियोगो लैंगिकः ॥

*Gist:* *ā na iḷābhiḥ* is the seventh sūkta of Anuvāka 24, of eleven ṛks, by Agastya, to the Viśve Devāḥ, in *triṣṭubh*; its use (*viniyoga*) is *laiṅgika*.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** The sūkta beginning with अनुवादवु—आ न इळाभिः ऎंब (*anuvādavu—ā na iḷābhiḥ èṃba*) is the seventh sūkta in the twenty-fourth anuvāka. It has eleven ṛks. Agastya is the seer of this sūkta; the Viśve Devāḥ are the deity; the metre is *triṣṭubh*. The Anukramaṇikā says अनुक्रमणिकॆयल्लि—आ नो वैश्वदेवं (*anukramaṇikèyalli—ā no vaiśvadevaṃ*) ("*ā no vaiśvadevam*"). The use of this sūkta is *laiṅgika* (by the indications of the text).

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> आ न॒ इळा॑भिर्वि॒दथे॑ सुश॒स्ति वि॒श्वान॑रः सवि॒ता दे॒व ए॑तु ।
> अपि॒ यथा॑ युवानो॒ मत्स॑था नो॒ विश्वं॒ जग॑दभिपि॒त्वे म॑नी॒षा ॥ १ ॥

> *ā na iḷābhirvidathe suśasti viśvānaraḥ savitā deva etu |*
> *api yathā yuvāno matsathā no viśvaṃ jagadabhipitve manīṣā ‖ 1 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads हडहारः (*haḍahāraḥ*) (inside the Saṃhitā block); standard label inserted. The Saṃhitā lines were joined.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> आ । नः । इळाभिः । विदथे । सुशस्ति । विश्वानरः । सविता । देवः । एतु ।
> अपि । यथा । युवानः । मत्सथ । नः । विश्वं । जगत् । अभिपित्वे । मनीषा ॥ १ ॥

> *ā | naḥ | iḷābhiḥ | vidathe | suśasti | viśvānaraḥ | savitā | devaḥ | etu |*
> *api | yathā | yuvānaḥ | matsatha | naḥ | viśvaṃ | jagat | abhipitve | manīṣā ‖ 1 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads सायुणभाष्यं (*sāyuṇabhāṣyaṃ*) (inside the Pada block); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> नो विदथेऽस्मदीये यज्ञे सुशस्ति ॥ सुपो लुक् ॥ सुशस्तिभिः शोभन-
> शंसनैः स्तुतिभिः प्रदेयै रत्नैः सह । यद्वा । इळाभिः । तदाश्रितेषु ताच्छब्दं । भूमि-
> स्थानाभिर्देवताभिः सह । विश्वानरः सर्वप्राणिहितकार्यग्निः सविता प्रेरक आदित्यश्च
> देव एतु । आगच्छतु । हे युवानो नित्यतरुणा यज्ञस्य मिश्रयितारो वा यूयं
> नोऽस्मानभिपित्वेऽभिपतनीयेऽस्मद्यज्ञॆ मनीषा मनीषया मनस ईशितृत्वेन ।
> स्वच्छयेत्यर्थः । अपिः संभावनायां । अप्यस्मान्मत्सथ । मादयथ । यथा विश्वं
> जगत् । एतत्सा वरस्याप्युपलक्षणं । यद्वा । कृत्स्नं प्रपंचं मत्सथ तथास्मानपि ।
> यद्वा । जगच्छब्दो यजमानरूपमनुष्यवाची । सर्वानपि यजमानान्यथा मत्सथ
> तथेत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* May Viśvānara Agni and Savitṛ come to our sacrifice with the earth-deities and with fine praises and gifts; you gods, delight us as you delight the whole world.

### Printed p. 101

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 101 (as supplied).*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

विश्वानरः (*viśvānaraḥ*) — Agni, beneficent to the whole world. सविता देवः (*savitā devaḥ*) — and the divine Savitṛ. इळाभिः (*iḷābhiḥ*) — together with the earth-deities. नः विदथे (*naḥ vidathe*) — to this our sacrifice. सुशस्ति (*suśasti*) — with excellent gifts. एतु (*etu*) — may (he) come. युवानः (*yuvānaḥ*) — O ever-young Viśve Devāḥ. अभिपित्वे (*abhipitve*) — at the sacrifice being performed forthwith. मनीषा (*manīṣā*) — by your own pleasure. यथा विश्वं (*yathā viśvaṃ*) — as (you gladden) जगत् (*jagat*) the whole world, or all the sacrificers. नः अपि (*naḥ api*) — us too. मत्सथ (*matsatha*) — gladden.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads भाषार्थ (*bhāṣārtha*); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May Agni, beneficent to the whole world, and the divine Savitṛ come, together with the earth-deities, to this our sacrifice with excellent gifts. O ever-young Viśve Devāḥ, at the sacrifice being performed forthwith, gladden us also by your own pleasure, as you gladden all the sacrificers.

**Printed English translation**

May the divine Savitri, the benefactor of all men, come benignantly to our solemniy [sic], togther [sic] with the divinities of earth, and do you, who are (always) young, willingly present at our sacrifice, lexhilarate [sic] us, as (you exhilarate) the whole world.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

*[The words discussed below do not occur in the Saṃhitā of Ṛk 1 given above; they are translated as supplied.]*

सजोषाः—जुषी (*sajoṣāḥ—juṣī*) — for this word, derived from the root सजोषाः—जुषी (*sajoṣāḥ—juṣī*) ("*juṣ*", in the senses of pleasure and service), Yāska in his Nirukta, having quoted the ṛk

अजुह्वान ईड्यो वंद्यश्चायाह्यग्ने वसुभिः सजोषाः । (*ajuhvāna īḍyo vaṃdyaścāyāhyagne vasubhiḥ sajoṣāḥ |*)
त्वं देवनामसि यह्व होता स एनान् यक्षीषितो यजीयान् ॥ (*tvaṃ devanāmasi yahva hotā sa enān yakṣīṣito yajīyān ‖*)

((म. सं. १०-११०-३) (*(ma. saṃ. 10-110-3)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

explains: सजोषाः-सह जोषणः (समानप्रीतिर्भूत्वा) (*sajoṣāḥ-saha joṣaṇaḥ (samānaprītirbhūtvā)*), that is, "being of equal pleasure"; this is his explanation of the meaning.

शवः— (*śavaḥ—*) — the word शवः (*śavaḥ*) is said among the names of water ((नि. २-२४) (*(ni. 2-24)*)) and among the names of strength ((नि. ३-९) (*(ni. 3-9)*)); so शवः (*śavaḥ*) means "strength". Here the bhāṣyakāra has taken it as food that produces strength (nourishing).

### Printed p. 102

*Running head: [102 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186]. The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 of Sūkta 186 from p. 101.*

विश्वे (*viśve*) (the Viśve Devāḥ) — this name is read among the thirty-one names that denote special deities such as the Aśvins. The bhāṣyakāra says that these are separate special deities. Some say they are all the gods. But from what is said in other sūktas it can be stated plainly that the ṛṣis worshipped the Viśve Devāḥ as separate deities. On this matter Yāska has written an explanation of the ṛk ओमासः (*omāsaḥ*):

अवितारो वावनीया वा मनुष्यधृतः सर्वॆ देवा इहागच्छत दत्तवंतो दत्तवतः सुतमिति, तदेततेकमेव वैश्वदेवं गायत्रं तृचं दशतयीषु विद्यते यत्तु किंचि- द्वहुदैवतं तद्वैश्वदेवानां स्थाने युज्यते यदेव विश्वलिंगमिति (*avitāro vāvanīyā vā manuṣyadhṛtaḥ sarvè devā ihāgacchata dattavaṃto dattavataḥ sutamiti, tadetatekameva vaiśvadevaṃ gāyatraṃ tṛcaṃ daśatayīṣu vidyate yattu kiṃci- dvahudaivataṃ tadvaiśvadevānāṃ sthāne yujyate yadeva viśvaliṃgamiti*)

(नि. १२-४०) (*(ni. 12-40)*) In the sense of this ṛk there is no difference between the meaning we have given and Yāska's meaning. But, according to Yāska, *viśve devāḥ* means "all the gods", while the Viśve Devāḥ as a special group of deities called by that name is the opinion of one Śākapūṇi, as Yāska himself says. It can therefore be said firmly that already in his time there was a difference of opinion on this matter. As is the custom today in domestic and similar rites, these Viśve Devāḥ are not of the group of the common gods but a special group of thirteen persons. Be that as it may, it seems right to say that the Viśve Devāḥ described in the Ṛgveda belong to a separate group of deities.

In the Ṛgveda there are 63 sūktas that praise the Viśve Devāḥ alone. Besides, in the sūkta १-१६४ (*1-164*) the first 41 ṛks are addressed to the Viśve Devāḥ. In all of these, except the sūkta ऋ सं. १०-१५५ (*ṛ saṃ. 10-155*), many deities, that is, Agni, Indra and other gods, as in other sūktas, are praised, and besides them trees, rivers, mountains, cows, horses and the like are also celebrated. Apart from these, in sūktas of other deities, in many places a total of 17 ṛks are addressed to the Viśve Devāḥ. In all these places the Viśve Devāḥ themselves are named as the deity of the ṛks. And in the mantra ऋ. सं. १०-१२५-१ (*ṛ. saṃ. 10-125-1*) the Vasus, Rudras and Ādityas are included along with the Viśve Devāḥ.

In the mantra २-२२ (*2-22*) of the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā (Śukla Yajurveda) Indra, the Ādityas, the Vasus and the Maruts are prayed to join the Viśve Devāḥ. In the mantras ३३-४५ रिंद ४७ (*33-45 riṃda 47*) of the same Saṃhitā, Indra, Vāyu and other gods are praised together with the Viśve Devāḥ.

In the mantra २०-६२-६ (*20-62-6*) of the Atharvaveda the Viśve Devāḥ are indicated in the singular, and there are many sūktas in the Atharvaveda addressed to the Viśve Devāḥ.

### Printed p. 103

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 103 (as supplied).*

In the Brāhmaṇas too the name of the Viśve Devāḥ is mentioned here and there. In the Kauṣītaki Brāhmaṇa (४-१४ (*4-14*)) and the Gopatha Brāhmaṇa (१-२० (*1-20*)) all the gods are addressed by the very name Viśve Devāḥ. In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa (३-९-२-६ (*3-9-2-6*)) the Viśve Devāḥ are called the rays of the sun, in Śat. Brā. १४-२-२-३७ (*14-2-2-37*) the vital airs such as *prāṇa*, in Śat. Brā. ७-१-१-४२ (*7-1-1-42*) the sacrificial rites, and in Śat. Brā. २-४-४-१३ (*2-4-4-13*) Indra and Agni. In the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka (तै. आ. ५-१-६ (*tai. ā. 5-1-6*)) Indra and Agni are praised together with the Viśve Devāḥ. In some places in the Purāṇas the Viśve Devāḥ are said to be the lords of the quarters.

In the Nirukta (नि. १२-३९ (*ni. 12-39*)) it is said that the Viśve Devāḥ are all the gods. Durgācārya, the commentator of the Nirukta, has explained the Viśve Devāḥ as the rays of the sun. Yāska, the author of the Nirukta, has stated his opinion that the Viśve Devāḥ are all the gods, and has also indicated the opinion of Śākapūṇi. Śaunaka, in his work the Bṛhaddevatā (बृ. दे. २-१४८ रिंद १३४ (*bṛ. de. 2-148 riṃda 134*), verses), speaking of the Viśve Devāḥ, says that Yāska and Śāṇḍilya hold that *viśve devāḥ* means all the gods. Śaunaka's view is the same.

Sāyaṇācārya, in commenting on this ṛk, has expressed his opinion that the Viśve Devāḥ are a group of thirty-one gods beginning with the Aśvins, called Viśve Devāḥ together.

Among Western scholars, Langlois, Kaegi and Hopkins hold that the Viśve Devāḥ are all the gods; Wilson, Griffith and Macdonell accept both meanings: all the gods, or a separate group of gods called the Viśve Devāḥ.

The main point is this: in the earliest, very ancient time the meaning "all the gods" was current for the Viśve Devāḥ, and in course of time it came into use to regard the Viśve Devāḥ as a separate group of gods of that name. In many Brāhmaṇas and Purāṇas the Viśve Devāḥ are indicated as a separate group of gods. But that even in Yāska's time there was a difference of opinion on this matter is known from Yāska's Nirukta itself.

In the Ṛgveda, besides the ṛks to the Viśve Devāḥ that occur here and there, there are 58 separate sūktas addressed to the Viśve Devāḥ: in the first Maṇḍala 14, 89, 90, 105, 106 (printed "१.०६ (*1.06*)"), 107, 121, and (the next page begins) 12 [as printed]

### Printed p. 104

*Running head: [104 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186].*

sūktas; in the second Maṇḍala 29, 31; in the third Maṇḍala 54; in the fourth Maṇḍala 55; in the fifth Maṇḍala 41, 42, 43, 44, 45, 47, 48, 49, 50, 51; in the sixth Maṇḍala 49, 50, 51, 52; in the seventh Maṇḍala 35, 36, 37, 39, 40, 42, 43; in the eighth Maṇḍala 27, 28, 29, 30, 58, 83; in the tenth Maṇḍala 31, 32, 35, 36, 64, 65, 66, 92, 93, 100, 101, 109, 114, 126, 128, 137, 141, 157, 181: these sūktas are addressed to the Viśve Devāḥ.

विदथे—यज्ञे (*vidathe—yajñe*) — यज्ञदल्लि (*yajñadalli*) ("at the sacrifice"), that is, "at the place of sacrifice". By वेदयत्सु स्तोत्रेषु निमित्तभूतेषु (*vedayatsu stotreṣu nimittabhūteṣu*) ("in the praises that are the occasion of making known") the derivation of the word *vidatha* is shown. Here only one of the derivations of the Nirukta-author is followed. यज्ञः, वेनः (*yajñaḥ, venaḥ*) ("yajña, vena" and so on) are read among the names of sacrifice ((नि ३-१९), (*(ni 3-19),*) as printed), and विधथानि वेदनानि (*vidhathāni vedanāni*) (*vidathāni* = *vedanāni*, "knowings") — to show that the word *vidatha* means "knowledge" ((नि. ६-७). (*(ni. 6-7).*)).

होता देवो अमत्यर्ति पुरस्तादेति मायया । विदथानि प्रचोदयन् ॥ (*hotā devo amatyarti purastādeti māyayā | vidathāni pracodayan ‖*)
(ऋ. सं. ३-२७-७) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 3-27-7)*)

having quoted this ṛk, he has shown that in it the word is formed from the root *vid* in the sense of knowing. Here Sāyaṇa has explained विदथानि वेदितव्यानि कर्माणि (*vidathāni veditavyāni karmāṇi*) as "rites that are to be known".

Some scholars have tried to give the exact meaning of this word, and each has given his own personal opinion. Since each has shown a different derivation of this word and given different meanings, it is seen that all these meanings fit according to the context. The scholar Max Müller, in explaining the ṛk

अमादेषां भियसा भूमिरेजति नर्न पूर्णा क्षरति व्यथिर्यती। (*amādeṣāṃ bhiyasā bhūmirejati narna pūrṇā kṣarati vyathiryatī|*)
दॊरेदृशो ये चितयंत एमभिरंतर्महे विदथे येतिरे नरः ॥ (*dòredṛśo ye citayaṃta emabhiraṃtarmahe vidathe yetire naraḥ ‖*)

((म. सं. ५-५९-२) (*(ma. saṃ. 5-59-2)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

has explained the word *vidathe* as "(sacrificial) assembly" (printed in English: "the great sacrificial assembly"). And in giving his explanation, after stating the meanings that other German scholars have given to this word and their opinions on its root, he concludes at the end: "Vidatha may have been the name of a sacred act. as Veda is of sacred knowledge. But the fact remains that it is best translated by assembly, particularly an assembly for sacrificial purposes." (Sacred Books of the East, Vol. 32, p. 349-50; the reference is printed twice in the supplied text.)

Some have said that a sacred act is the meaning of the word *vidatha*, as sacred knowledge is the meaning of the word *veda*. But he has concluded that there is no doubt that "sacrificial assembly" is the chief meaning of this word.

### Printed p. 105

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 105 (as supplied, "ಅ ೫"). The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 of Sūkta 186 (the word *vidatha*) from p. 104.*

The scholar Hermann Oldenberg, in his work *Vedic Hymns*, in explaining the word विदथे (*vidathe*) in the ṛk १-३१-६ने (*1-31-6ne*) [as printed], has stated his opinion in this way. His words are quoted and then his opinions are stated.

On the derivation and meaning of विदथ (*vidatha*) Various opinions have been prononnced [sic] in the last years which have been collected by prof. Max Muller in bi- note on R. V V-59-2 (S. B. E. Vol. 32 p. 349 sq.) without trying to discuss here all different theories, I proceed to state my own opinion- I propose to derive विदथ (*vidatha*) from वि (*vi*)+धा (*dhā*); The Verb वि (*vi*)+धा (*dhā*) means 'to distribute, to arrange, to ordain;' thus the original meaning of विदथ (*vidatha*) must be like the meaning of विधान (*vidhāna*), 'distribution, disposition, ordinance; In V. 3-6, we read विदथेषु आह्नां (*vidatheṣu āhnāṃ*): this phrase receives its explanation by VII. 66-11 we may call attention also to VI. 51-2 within the sphere of the Vedic poets ' thoughts, the most prominent example of something most artificially विहित (*vihita*) was the secrifice [sic]. यज्ञ (*yajña*) and विदथ (*vidatha*), Sacrifice and ordinance became nearly synony-mous. (III 3·3) .... .... .... .... finally विदथ (*vidatha*) seems to mean 'the act of disposing of any business' or the like; this meaning appears, I believe, in some passages like the well-known phrase, बृहद्वदेम विदथे (*bṛhadvadema vidathe*) 'ऎवीरा (*èvīrā*):-may we with Valiant men mightily raise our voice in the determining of ordinances. Thus the word विदथ (*vidatha*) and सभा (*sabhā*) approach each other in their meaning; a person influential in council is called hoth विदथ (*vidatha*), and सभेय (*sabheya*) '.

(Sacred Books of the East. Vol. 46. p. 27)

The main substance of this writing is this: the word *vidatha* is formed by adding the prefix *vi* to the root *dhā*. *Vidhā* means "distribute, ordain"; it may also mean "observance of rule". That the meanings of the two words *vidatha* and *vidhā* are the same is understood from the equal sense of the words विदथेष्वह्नां (*vidatheṣvahnāṃ*) in the ṛk

वयमग्ने वनुयाम त्वोता वसूयवो हविषा बुध्यमानाः । (*vayamagne vanuyāma tvotā vasūyavo haviṣā budhyamānāḥ |*)
वयं समर्ये विदथेष्वह्नां वयं राया सहसस्पुत्र मर्तान् ॥ (*vayaṃ samarye vidatheṣvahnāṃ vayaṃ rāyā sahasasputra martān ‖*)

((म. सं. ५-३-६) (*(ma. saṃ. 5-3-6)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

and of the words वि-दधुः (*vi-dadhuḥ*) in the ṛk

वि ये दधुः शरदं मासमादहरज्ञ मक्तुं चादृचं । (*vi ye dadhuḥ śaradaṃ māsamādaharajña maktuṃ cādṛcaṃ |*)
अनाप्यं वरुणो मित्रो अरमा क्षत्रं राजान आशत ॥ (*anāpyaṃ varuṇo mitro aramā kṣatraṃ rājāna āśata ‖*)

((ऋ. सं.७-६६-११) (*(ṛ. saṃ.7-66-11)*))

And the meanings of the words *vihita* and *vidatha* being one, the sacrifice enjoined by the śāstra came to be called by the name *vidatha*

### Printed p. 106

*Running head: [106 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 186].*

and the meaning "sacrifice" or "sacrificial assembly" remained at the end: thus this scholar has explained.

Bloomfield has shown the faults in the meanings given by Max Müller and Oldenberg, saying that *vidatha* never means "assembly" and that the meaning "private house" is the right one. His opinion is as follows:

The word विदथ (*vidatha*) contains the root विद् (*vid*) in its ordinary sense of 'get, acquire'. Instead of this, the radical idea underlying the word was generally felt to be 'come to-gether,' 'assemble'. Again, the false start from the notion of 'assemblage' resulted in an approxi-mation of the word to other words for assemblage, especially to सभा (*sabhā*) with which विदथ (*vidatha*) was almost identified. We shall see that there are decided points of contact between the words विदथ (*vidatha*) and सभा (*sabhā*) but the contact is that of extreme opposites in a way. विदथ (*vidatha*) refers to home matters; सभा (*sabhā*) generally to public matters.

In the interpretation of a term that figures prominently in the mystic—hieratic sphere of the Veda it is peculiarly necessary to search for its uses outside of that sphere, if there be such uses. In X—85-26, the bride after the wedding ceremony proper, as the newly married couple are about to travel to their new home is addressed with the words "May Pushan taking thee hy tbe [sic] hand lead thee home, may the Aswins lead thee forth on their car. Go to your house in order that thou mayst be mistress of the house ".

Now विदथ (*vidatha*) here, can but have one meaning, 'household' or something very like it. It certainly cannot refer to anything in the nature of a public gathering similar to the सभा (*sabhā*).

In the phrase बृहद्वदेम विदधे सुवीराः (*bṛhadvadema vidadhe suvīrāḥ*) (R. V. 2-1-16), the word सुवीराः (*suvīrāḥ*) refers to family relations not to public life.

Thus far, it may not be doubted, the word appears in the language of ordinary life-

But it is found to a very much greater extent in the sacerdotal sphere: indeed, as all interpreters from Yaska on, have agreed, it often has a meaning very close to Yagna 'Sacrifice ' itself. This is as it should be. In as much as the sacrifice is a private, home affair, it is very natural विदथ (*vidatha*) the house, the premises, the establishment, should figure prominently in connection with the sacrifice.

The summary of this writing is that it is not right that some have given the sense "sabhā" to the word विदथ (*vidatha*). The meaning of this word is "private house". Use of the word in this meaning can be noticed in many places. For example:

पूषा त्वेतो नयतु हस्तगृह्याश्विना त्वा प्र वहतां रथेन । (*pūṣā tveto nayatu hastagṛhyāśvinā tvā pra vahatāṃ rathena |*)

### Printed p. 107

*Running head: [Ā. 2. A. 5. Va. 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 107 (as supplied, "ಆ. ೨. ಅ. ೫. ವ. .೪").*

गृहान् गच्छ गृहपत्नी यथासो वशिनी त्वं विदथवा वदासि ॥ (*gṛhān gaccha gṛhapatnī yathāso vaśinī tvaṃ vidathavā vadāsi ‖*)
((म सं. १०-८५-२६) (*(ma saṃ. 10-85-26)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ ಸಂ.")

In this ṛk it is plain that the sense is that Pūṣan and the Aśvin deities should lead you safely to the husband's house. And further:

ये स्तोतृभ्यो गोअग्रामश्वपेशसमग्ने रातिमुपसृजंति सूरयः । (*ye stotṛbhyo goagrāmaśvapeśasamagne rātimupasṛjaṃti sūrayaḥ |*)
अस्माञ्च तांश्च प्र हि नेषि वस्य आ बृहद्वदेम विदथे सुवीराः ॥ (*asmāñca tāṃśca pra hi neṣi vasya ā bṛhadvadema vidathe suvīrāḥ ‖*)
((म. सं. २१-१६) (*(ma. saṃ. 21-16)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

in this ṛk also, since *suvīrāḥ* is explained as "accompanied by good sons and so on", the sense is "house". But since sacrificial work is done in the house, here it comes to be "sacrificial house". The same sense lies in what Yāska and others have said, that it is a synonym of *yajña*. Thus many scholars among the Westerners have given their own personal opinions.

Since the different meanings house, sacrifice and assembly all fit, none of them can be called wrong. All these meanings are found in the Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya itself. For example, in the ṛks १०-८५-२६ मत्तु २७ (*10-85-26 mattu 27*) and ७-८४-३ (*7-84-3*) and the like he has explained it as "house", and in १०-१००-६ (*10-100-6*) and १०-११०-७ (*10-110-7*) and many other ṛks as "sacrifice". It also means "sacrificial house" and "sacrificial assembly".

इळाभिः— (*iḷābhiḥ—*) — the word *iḷā* is read among the names of the earth (नि. २-५ (*ni. 2-5*)), of speech (नि. २-२३ (*ni. 2-23*)), of food (नि ३-९ (*ni 3-9*)) and of the cow (नि. ३-९ (*ni. 3-9*)); so the word *iḷā* has the different meanings earth, speech, food and cow. Here the bhāṣyakāra has taken the meaning "earth" and explained इळाभिः—भूमिस्थानाभिर्देवताभिः (*iḷābhiḥ—bhūmisthānābhirdevatābhiḥ*), that is, "by the deities who dwell on the earth".

विश्वानरः—सर्वप्राणिहितकार्यग्निः (*viśvānaraḥ—sarvaprāṇihitakāryagniḥ*) — "Agni, who does good to all beings": this is the bhāṣyakāra's explanation. Yāska in his Nirukta has said:

वैश्वानरः । वैश्वानरः कस्माद्विश्वान्नरान्नयति । विश्वएनं नरा नयंतीति वा । (*vaiśvānaraḥ | vaiśvānaraḥ kasmādviśvānnarānnayati | viśvaenaṃ narā nayaṃtīti vā |*)
अपि ना विश्वानर एव स्यात् प्रत्यृतः सर्वाणि भूतानि तस्य वैश्वानरः । (*api nā viśvānara eva syāt pratyṛtaḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni tasya vaiśvānaraḥ |*)
((नि. ७-२१) (*(ni. 7-21)*))

Asking "What is Vaiśvānara? Why has this deity this name?", he gives the answer that this deity (Agni) is called Vaiśvānara because he leads all, that is, is the guide of all, or because in sacrifices and the like the sacrificer and priests carry this Agni from the old altar to the place of the Āhavanīya fire. And this Agni (all living beings

*[The window ends here, in the middle of the sentence; the next window continues from "ಸಕಲ ಪ್ರಾಣಿಗಳನ್ನೂ".]*

### Printed p. 108

*Running head: [108 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 18] (the supplied text is cut at "೧೮"). The window continues the Vaiśvānara note of the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 of Sūkta 186 from p. 107; the sentence is completed here.*

... enters all living beings, that is, because he has entered all living beings in the form of the digestive fire (*jāṭharāgni*), this Agni is named Vaiśvānara; they have given the derivation "*viśvānara* himself is *vaiśvānara*". For the word *vaiśvānara* they have given the derivation in three ways: first, विश्वे सर्वॆ नरा एनमग्निं यज्ञादौ प्रणयंतीति विश्वेनराः तत्संबंधाद्वैश्वानरः (*viśve sarvè narā enamagniṃ yajñādau praṇayaṃtīti viśvenarāḥ tatsaṃbaṃdhādvaiśvānaraḥ*) ("all men lead this Agni in sacrifices and the like, so they are *viśve-narāḥ*; from connection with them, *vaiśvānara*"); second, विश्वेषां नराणां लोकांतरं प्रति नेतृतया संबंधी वैश्वानरः (*viśveṣāṃ narāṇāṃ lokāṃtaraṃ prati netṛtayā saṃbaṃdhī vaiśvānaraḥ*) ("he who is connected, as leader, with all men with regard to the other world, is *vaiśvānara*"); and third, विश्वान् सर्वान् प्राणिनः प्रत्यतो गच्छत इति वैश्वानरौ, मध्यमोत्तमौ, ताभ्यामुत्पन्न 8 वैश्वानरः (*viśvān sarvān prāṇinaḥ pratyato gacchata iti vaiśvānarau, madhyamottamau, tābhyāmutpanna 8 vaiśvānaraḥ*) ("*vaiśvānarau* are the middle and the last; one produced from those two is *vaiśvānara*"; the numeral "8" stands in the supplied text before *vaiśvānaraḥ* and is kept as supplied). The sense expressed is the praise of the greatness of Vaiśvānara, who killed Vṛtra the cloud-obstructer, gives rain and destroys demons. The Nirukta that points to this same purport is as follows:

प्रब्रमीमि तन्महित्वं महाभाग्यं वृषभस्य वर्षितुरपां यं पूरवः पूरयितव्या मनुष्या वृत्रहणं मेघहनं सचंते सेवंते वर्षकामा दस्युर्दस्यते क्षयार्थादुपदस्यंत्यस्मिन् रसा उपदासयति कर्माणि तमग्निर्वैश्वा- नरो घन्न वाधुनोदपः काष्ठा अभिनच्छंबरं मेघं (*prabramīmi tanmahitvaṃ mahābhāgyaṃ vṛṣabhasya varṣiturapāṃ yaṃ pūravaḥ pūrayitavyā manuṣyā vṛtrahaṇaṃ meghahanaṃ sacaṃte sevaṃte varṣakāmā dasyurdasyate kṣayārthādupadasyaṃtyasmin rasā upadāsayati karmāṇi tamagnirvaiśvā- naro ghanna vādhunodapaḥ kāṣṭhā abhinacchaṃbaraṃ meghaṃ*)

((निरु. ७-२०) (*(niru. 7-20)*), "ನಿರು." as printed.) After this they have inquired who the Vaiśvānara spoken of here is. Then some have said that the Vaiśvānara should be Vāyu or Indra, who stand in the middle region, because they two have authority over the work of rain; in the matter of getting rain it is they whom one must generally praise; since Agni is said to have the earth as his place, this is not possible, they have argued. Others say that the word *vaiśvānara* spoken of here indicates the Sun, because the morning, midday and evening pressings (*savana*) are set out in order as the Earth world, the Atmosphere world and the Heaven world, and it is concluded that by performing those pressings those worlds are obtained. Then by the performance of the third pressing, heaven is obtained, and a complete falling away from the earth occurs. To remove such a falling away they recite the mantra of the last śastra, the Agni-Mārutam, so as to come back to the earth. That śastra-mantra begins with the Vaiśvānara sūkta. After that, the Rudra deity and the Maruts connected with the middle region are caused to descend by the recitation of the sūkta of those deities. Since Agni has the earth as his place, if the word *vaiśvānara* here did not mean the Sun, how could the descent to the earth be connected? Having so concluded, they have explained *vaiśvānara* as the Sun. But Sāyaṇācārya's opinion is that the word *vaiśvānara* means the Agni of the middle region and related to "ನಿಮ್ಮ…" [the supplied text has a broken word here, निम्म (*nimma*) at the line end and त्संबंधवाद (*tsaṃbaṃdhavāda*) at the next line start, which I cannot settle; ?].

**The greatness of Vaiśvānara Agni** (printed heading वैश्वानराग्नियॆ महिमॆ (*vaiśvānarāgniyè mahimè*))

इंद्रं मित्रं वरुणमग्निमाहुरथो दिव्यः स सुपर्णो गरुत्मान् । (*iṃdraṃ mitraṃ varuṇamagnimāhuratho divyaḥ sa suparṇo garutmān |*)
एकं सद्विप्रा बहुधा वदंत्यग्निं यमं मातरिश्वानमाहुः ॥ (*ekaṃ sadviprā bahudhā vadaṃtyagniṃ yamaṃ mātariśvānamāhuḥ ‖*)

((म. सं. १-१६४-९६) (*(ma. saṃ. 1-164-96)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

### Printed p. 109

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 109 (as supplied).*

इममेवाग्निं महांतमात्मानमेकमात्मानं बहुधा मेधाविनो वदंति ॥ (*imamevāgniṃ mahāṃtamātmānamekamātmānaṃ bahudhā medhāvino vadaṃti ‖*) ("Thoughtful men speak in many ways of this very one great Self, Agni.") अग्निः सर्वा देवताः इति ब्राह्मणमपि भवति. (*agniḥ sarvā devatāḥ iti brāhmaṇamapi bhavati.*) ("There is a Brāhmaṇa text also: 'Agni is all the deities'") ((नि. ७-१८) (*(ni. 7-18)*)). The great Self, the Self of all, this Agni alone is addressed by various names such as Indra and worshipped by all. And there are statements of the Śruti that this Vaiśvānara Agni is the source of all the portions of Agni that appear in various circumstances in other forms, and that all the other forms are branches of this Vaiśvānara Agni.

वया इदग्ने अग्नयस्ते अन्वे त्वे विश्वे अमृता मादयंते । (*vayā idagne agnayaste anve tve viśve amṛtā mādayaṃte |*)
वैश्वानर नाभिरसि क्षितीनां स्तूणेव जना उपमिद्ययंथ ॥ (*vaiśvānara nābhirasi kṣitīnāṃ stūṇeva janā upamidyayaṃtha ‖*)
((म. सं. १-५९-१) (*(ma. saṃ. 1-59-1)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

The Vaiśvānara Agni is the cause of the existence of the whole world of gods, men and the rest, and is the leader of all the dealings of the world; therefore all beings who desire their own welfare and the welfare of the world must know the nature of this Vaiśvānara Agni, the Self of all, and worship him; the Śruti texts that teach the performance of rites and the Śruti texts that teach spiritual knowledge alike declare the necessity of the worship of Vaiśvānara Agni. The Saṃhitās, the Ṛgveda and others, say वैश्वानरस्य सुमतौ स्याम (ऋ. सं. (*vaiśvānarasya sumatau syāma (ṛ. saṃ.*) १-९८-१) (*1-98-1)*) ("may we be in the good graces of Vaiśvānara"); the Brāhmaṇas such as the Śatapatha say स यो ह्येतमेवमग्निं वैश्वानरं पुरुषविधं पुरुषेऽंतः प्रतिष्ठितं वेदाप पुनर्म्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायुरेति (*sa yo hyetamevamagniṃ vaiśvānaraṃ puruṣavidhaṃ puruṣe 'ṃtaḥ pratiṣṭhitaṃ vedāpa punarmmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyureti*) — "whoever knows Vaiśvānara Agni, who is of the form of a man and abides within man, conquers death again and attains the whole life" — that all who know the nature of this Vaiśvānara Agni, who is hidden within man and has the form of man, conquer death and obtain full life ((श. ब्रा. १०-६-१-११) (*(śa. brā. 10-6-1-11)*)); and the Upaniṣads such as the Chāndogya have declared the greatness of the worship of Vaiśvānara Agni: अथ य एतदेवं विद्वानग्नि होत्रं जुहोति तस्य सर्वॆषु लोकेषु सर्वॆषु भूतेषु सर्वॆष्वात्मसु हुतं भवति (*atha ya etadevaṃ vidvānagni hotraṃ juhoti tasya sarvèṣu lokeṣu sarvèṣu bhūteṣu sarvèṣvātmasu hutaṃ bhavati*) ("then he who, knowing this, performs the Agnihotra, his offering is made in all worlds, in all beings, in all selves"); that is, the man who, knowing the nature of Vaiśvānara Agni, performs the Agnihotra offers as it were in all beings, all worlds and all selves ((छा. उ. ५-२४-२) (*(chā. u. 5-24-2)*)). Therefore it is most necessary in this context to know what the nature of this deity is, and what the relation of this deity is to the performance of rites and the acquisition of spiritual knowledge. In the above ṛk (राजा हि कं भुवनानामभिश्रीः । (*rājā hi kaṃ bhuvanānāmabhiśrīḥ |*) "for he is the king, the lustre of the worlds") Vaiśvānara is described as the venerable lord of all beings. But the question who that Vaiśvānara is arises at the same time, and the author of the Nirukta, in explaining this very ṛk, has taken many examples and concluded that Vaiśvānara is the terrestrial Agni. His explanation is as follows:

वैश्वानरः कस्माद्विश्वान्नरान्नयति । विश्व एनं नरा नयंतीति वा । (*vaiśvānaraḥ kasmādviśvānnarānnayati | viśva enaṃ narā nayaṃtīti vā |*)

### Printed p. 110

*Running head: [110 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 186].*

अपि वा विश्वानर एव स्यात् प्रत्युतः सर्वाणि भूतानि तस्य वैश्वानरः ॥ (*api vā viśvānara eva syāt pratyutaḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni tasya vaiśvānaraḥ ‖*)
((नि. ७-२१-) (*(ni. 7-21-)*), as printed with a final hyphen)

Because he is the leader and guide of all, or because all take him as the chief deity in the sacrifice, or because he pervades everywhere and has entered all beings: so he explains the present ṛk वैश्वानरस्य सुमतौ स्याम (*vaiśvānarasya sumatau syāma*) and has made known the nature of this deity: इतो जातः सर्वमिदमभिपश्यति वैश्वानरः संयतते सूर्येण राजा यः । सर्वेषां भूतानामभिश्रयणीयस्तस्य वयं वैश्वानरस्य कल्याण्यां मत् सौ स्यामेति । (*ito jātaḥ sarvamidamabhipaśyati vaiśvānaraḥ saṃyatate sūryeṇa rājā yaḥ | sarveṣāṃ bhūtānāmabhiśrayaṇīyastasya vayaṃ vaiśvānarasya kalyāṇyāṃ mat sau syāmeti |*) ("he who, born from here, looks upon all this, Vaiśvānara, who goes together with the sun, the king: he is the refuge of all beings; may we be in the favour of that Vaiśvānara" [the Sanskrit as printed, with the broken words "ಮತ್ ಸೌ" kept]). That is: because he is the lord of all and the refuge of all, may we all be worthy of the gracious glance of this Vaiśvānara. Born in the form of the terrestrial Agni, he looks upon the whole world and becomes one with the Sun: so he explains the sense of the ṛk, and then, putting the question तत्को वैश्वानरः । (*tatko vaiśvānaraḥ |*) ("Who is Vaiśvānara?", (नि. ६-२२) (*(ni. 6-22)*)), discusses the disagreement of the traditions and at the end states his own considered opinion. In his discussion, first he points out the fault in the opinions which designate Vaiśvānara as a deity of the atmosphere.

मध्यम इत्याचार्याः । वर्षकर्मणा ह्येनं स्तौति । प्र नू महित्वं वृषभस्य वोचं । (*madhyama ityācāryāḥ | varṣakarmaṇā hyenaṃ stauti | pra nū mahitvaṃ vṛṣabhasya vocaṃ |*) ((ऋ. सं. १-५९-६) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-59-6)*)) — the teachers hold that he is a deity of the atmosphere because he is praised as the cause of the work of rain, as in the ṛk "I proclaim the greatness of the bull". Besides:

एषां लोकानां रोहेण सवनानां रोह आम्मातो रोहात् प्रत्यव- रोहश्चिकीर्षितस्तामनुकृतिं होताग्निमारुते शस्त्रे वैश्वानरीयेण सूक्तेन प्रतिपद्येते । (*eṣāṃ lokānāṃ roheṇa savanānāṃ roha āmmāto rohāt pratyava- rohaścikīrṣitastāmanukṛtiṃ hotāgnimārute śastre vaiśvānarīyeṇa sūktena pratipadyete |*)

सूरप्रसूतावग्नी तु दृष्टौ पार्थिवमध्यमौ । (*sūraprasūtāvagnī tu dṛṣṭau pārthivamadhyamau |*)
एतेषामेव लोकानां त्रयाणामध्वरेऽध्वरे ॥ (*eteṣāmeva lokānāṃ trayāṇāmadhvare 'dhvare ‖*)
रोहात्पत्यवरोहेण चिकीर्षन्नग्निमारुतं । (*rohātpatyavaroheṇa cikīrṣannagnimārutaṃ |*)
शस्त्रं वैश्वानरीयेण सूक्तेन प्रतिपद्यते ॥ (*śastraṃ vaiśvānarīyeṇa sūktena pratipadyate ‖*)
((बृ. दे. १-१०१ : १-१०२) (*(bṛ. de. 1-101 : 1-102)*), as printed with a colon between the two numbers)

There is a rule that the abundance of oblations should follow the order of ascent: earth, atmosphere and heaven. After the order of ascent, the order of descent must be carried out. Following this course the sacrificer, in this Vaiśvānarīya sūkta recited to Agni and the Maruts, praises Vaiśvānara first, in the order of descent. It is with this intention that here is indicated

*[The window ends here, in the middle of the sentence ("ಇಲ್ಲಿ ನಿರ್ದೇಶಿ…"); the next window continues from it.]*

### Printed p. 111

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 111 (as supplied, "ಅ. ೨ ಅ. ೫. ವ. ೪."). The window continues the Vaiśvānara discussion of the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 of Sūkta 186 from p. 110; the sentence is completed here.*

... the Vaiśvānara indicated here, अथासावादित्य इति पूर्वे यज्ञिकाः । (*athāsāvāditya iti pūrve yajñikāḥ |*) ("Now he is the Sun, say the early sacrificers"): the ancient sacrificers hold that he is Āditya. Having set out this prima facie view, he then says:

सोऽपि न स्तोत्रियमाद्रियताग्नेयो हि भवति । तत अगच्छति मध्यस्थाना देवता रुद्रं च मरुतश्च ततोग्निमिहस्थानमत्र वै स्तोत्रियं शंसति । (*so 'pi na stotriyamādriyatāgneyo hi bhavati | tata agacchati madhyasthānā devatā rudraṃ ca marutaśca tatognimihasthānamatra vai stotriyaṃ śaṃsati |*)

(The Sanskrit as printed.) Because this sūkta is addressed chiefly to Agni, and then, after indicating Rudra and the Maruts, the sacrificer again praises the very Agni belonging to the terrestrial world, in the terrestrial sacrificial hall where he recites this sūkta, the Vaiśvānara Agni indicated here is a deity of the earth, not of the atmosphere: thus they refute the above opinion. And in support of their position that this Vaiśvānara Agni is a terrestrial deity they have also quoted the appropriate Śruti passages:

अथाप्याह - इतो जातो विश्वमिदं वि चष्टे वैश्वानरो यतते सूर्येण ॥ (*athāpyāha - ito jāto viśvamidaṃ vi caṣṭe vaiśvānaro yatate sūryeṇa ‖*)
((ऋ. सं. १-९८-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-98-1)*))
न च पुनरात्मनात्मा संयततेऽन्येनैवान्यः संयतत इत इममादधात्यमु- तोऽमुष्य रश्मयः प्रादुर्भवंतीतोऽस्यार्चिषस्तयोर्भासोः संसंगं दृष्टैवमवक्ष्यत् ॥ (*na ca punarātmanātmā saṃyatate 'nyenaivānyaḥ saṃyatata ita imamādadhātyamu- to 'muṣya raśmayaḥ prādurbhavaṃtīto 'syārciṣastayorbhāsoḥ saṃsaṃgaṃ dṛṣṭaivamavakṣyat ‖*)

The statement of the Saṃhitā that Vaiśvānara spreads himself widely and joins the Sun helps this support. If Vaiśvānara were the Sun himself, it would be impossible that he should spread and join himself. It is natural to say that one thing extends and joins another. After the sacrificer has kindled Agni on the earth, its rays spread to the world above and appear there too. They join the rays of Āditya spread through the atmosphere, and, seeing the two become one, it is described by the words यतते सूरेयण (*yatate sūreyaṇa*) [?] [the supplied text reads this last word as printed, marked [?]]. And further:

अथ यान्येतान्यौत्तमिकानि सूक्तानि भागानि वा सावित्राणि वा सौर्याणि वा पौष्णानि वा वैष्णवानि वा वैश्वदेव्यानि वा तेषु वैश्वानरीयाः प्रवादा अभविष्यन्नादित्यकर्मणा चैनमस्तोष्यन्नित्युदेषीत्यस्तमेषीति विपरैषीति । (*atha yānyetānyauttamikāni sūktāni bhāgāni vā sāvitrāṇi vā sauryāṇi vā pauṣṇāni vā vaiṣṇavāni vā vaiśvadevyāni vā teṣu vaiśvānarīyāḥ pravādā abhaviṣyannādityakarmaṇā cainamastoṣyannityudeṣītyastameṣīti viparaiṣīti |*)

If Vaiśvānara were the Sun himself, then the forms and actions of the atmospheric deities, Savitṛ, Pūṣan, Viṣṇu, the Viśve Devāḥ and others, would have to be described in him by supplying them, and descriptions such as "he rises", "he sets", "he revolves" would have to occur. But

### Printed p. 112

*Running head: [112 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 186].*

here only descriptions addressed to Agni occur, so it is firmly established that he is a synonym of the terrestrial Agni. On these and other grounds,

यस्तु सूक्तं भजते यस्मै हविर्निरूप्यतेयमेव सोऽग्निर्वैश्वानरः । (*yastu sūktaṃ bhajate yasmai havirnirūpyateyameva so 'gnirvaiśvānaraḥ |*)

the praise recited in the sūkta and the offering of the oblation can be said with certainty to be offered to Agni in the form of Vaiśvānara ((नि. ७-२२ रिंद ७-३१) (*(ni. 7-22 riṃda 7-31)*), as printed). Even if the manifestation of this Vaiśvānara Agni is on the earth, he is the very one: राजा हि कं भुवनानामभिश्रीः । (*rājā hi kaṃ bhuvanānāmabhiśrīḥ |*) ("for he is the king, the lustre of the worlds") — the lord and refuge of all beings. Therefore it has been clearly stated in many places that gods, men and all can attain the highest good only by his worship. It is one method to know his terrestrial form through the performance of rites and to worship him; it is another kind of worship to be done through spiritual knowledge, knowing his greatness as all-pervading and indwelling in all things, moving and unmoving, of the whole world. The relation between the performance of rites and the worship by knowledge, and the greatness of Vaiśvānara Agni, the Self of all, is set out in the Śatapatha and other Brāhmaṇas and the Chāndogya and other Upaniṣads. The story of the ṛṣis who went to choose an excellent teacher to learn his true nature is told in the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa ((१०-६-१-१ रिंद ११) (*(10-6-1-1 riṃda 11)*), as printed).

अपि वा विश्वानर एव स्यात् प्रत्यृतः सर्वाणि भूतानि तस्य वैश्वानरः । (*api vā viśvānara eva syāt pratyṛtaḥ sarvāṇi bhūtāni tasya vaiśvānaraḥ |*)
(नि. ७-२१) (*(ni. 7-21)*) — As in the derivation by the Nirukta-author, "one who pervades everywhere and has entered all beings", this story makes known the nature of Viśvānara, the inner controller of all:

अथ हैतेऽरुणे औपवेशौ समाजग्मुः सत्ययज्ञः पौलुषिर्महाशालो जाबा- लो बुडिल आश्वतराश्विरिंद्रद्युम्नो भाल्लवेयो जनः शार्कराक्ष्यस्ते ह समासत तेषां ह वैश्वानरे न समियाय ॥१॥ (*atha haite 'ruṇe aupaveśau samājagmuḥ satyayajñaḥ pauluṣirmahāśālo jābā- lo buḍila āśvatarāśviriṃdradyumno bhāllaveyo janaḥ śārkarākṣyaste ha samāsata teṣāṃ ha vaiśvānare na samiyāya ‖1‖*)

Satyayajña Pauluṣi, Mahāśāla Jābāla, Buḍila Āśvatarāśvi, Indradyumna Bhāllaveya and Jana Śārkarākṣya together assembled in the house of Aruṇa Aupaveśi to learn the nature of Vaiśvānara Agni; though they all met and discussed, no firm understanding came to them, and a difference of opinion arose among them, a disagreement. ‖ 1 ‖

ते होचुः । अश्वपतिर्वायं कैकेयः संप्रति वैश्वानरं वेद तं गच्छामेति ते हाश्वपतिं कैकेयमाजग्मुस्तेभ्यो ह पृथगावसथान्पृथगपचितीः पृथक्- साहस्रान्सोमान्सोवाच ते ह प्रातरसंविदाना एव समित्पाणयः प्रति- चक्रमिर उपत्वायामेति ॥२॥ (*te hocuḥ | aśvapatirvāyaṃ kaikeyaḥ saṃprati vaiśvānaraṃ veda taṃ gacchāmeti te hāśvapatiṃ kaikeyamājagmustebhyo ha pṛthagāvasathānpṛthagapacitīḥ pṛthak- sāhasrānsomānsovāca te ha prātarasaṃvidānā eva samitpāṇayaḥ prati- cakramira upatvāyāmeti ‖2‖*)

### Printed p. 113

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 113 (as supplied).*

Then they all went to Aśvapati Kaikeya, who knew the nature of Vaiśvānara Agni fully. He honoured all these guests who had come, giving them separate lodgings, personal honours and thousands of drinks. Next morning, all of them, keeping their differences of opinion, took fuel sticks in their hands and entreated, "We wish to become your pupils." ‖ 2 ‖

स होवाच । यन्नु भगवंतोऽनूचाना अनूचानपुत्राः किमिदमति ते होचुर्वैश्वानरं ह भगवान्संप्रति वेद तं नो ब्रूहीति स होवाचॆ संप्रति खलु न्या [?] अहं वैश्वानरं वेदाभ्याधत्त समिध उपेता स्थेति ॥३॥ (*sa hovāca | yannu bhagavaṃto 'nūcānā anūcānaputrāḥ kimidamati te hocurvaiśvānaraṃ ha bhagavānsaṃprati veda taṃ no brūhīti sa hovācè saṃprati khalu nyā [?] ahaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vedābhyādhatta samidha upetā stheti ‖3‖*)

Then he, astonished, questioned them: "You are sons of men learned in all the śāstras, and have yourselves studied; why have you come to me as pupils?" Then they said: "You know the nature of Vaiśvānara Agni well; teach us also its secret." Then he said: "I know that secret and will tell you also. Offer fuel sticks to Agni; from today you are my pupils." ‖ 3 ‖ (The word न्या (*nyā*) [?] in the Sanskrit is doubtful in the supplied text and is kept as supplied, marked [?].)

स होवाचारुणमौपवेशिं । गौतम कं त्वं वैश्वानरं वेत्थेति पृथिवीमेव राजन्निति होवाचोमिति होवाचैष वै प्रतिष्ठा एतं रयिं वैश्वानर एतं हि वैश्वं प्रतिष्ठा वैश्वानरं वेत्थ तस्मात्त्वं प्र तिष्ठितः प्रज्ञया पशुभिरसि यो वा एतं प्रतिष्ठां वैश्वानरं वेदाप पुनर्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायु- रेति पादौ त्वा एतौ वैश्वानरस्य पादौ तेऽम्लास्यतां यदि ह नागमिष्य इति पादौ तेऽविदितावभविष्यतां यदि ह नागमिष्य इति वा ॥४॥ (*sa hovācāruṇamaupaveśiṃ | gautama kaṃ tvaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vettheti pṛthivīmeva rājanniti hovācomiti hovācaiṣa vai pratiṣṭhā etaṃ rayiṃ vaiśvānara etaṃ hi vaiśvaṃ pratiṣṭhā vaiśvānaraṃ vettha tasmāttvaṃ pra tiṣṭhitaḥ prajñayā paśubhirasi yo vā etaṃ pratiṣṭhāṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vedāpa punarmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyu- reti pādau tvā etau vaiśvānarasya pādau te 'mlāsyatāṃ yadi ha nāgamiṣya iti pādau te 'viditāvabhaviṣyatāṃ yadi ha nāgamiṣya iti vā ‖4‖*)

Then he asked Aruṇa Aupaveśi: "Gautama, whom do you know as Vaiśvānara?" "The earth, O king," he said. The king said, "Yes, it is the all-supporting Vaiśvānara. Because you know this secret, you stand firmly established with cattle, sons and so on! One who knows this conquers death and attains full life. But truly these are only the feet of Vaiśvānara. If you had not come here (to learn the full nature of Vaiśvānara), your feet would have become loose and wasted away, or would not have come into your view." ‖ 4 ‖

अथ होवाच सत्ययज्ञं पौलुषिं । प्राचीनयोग्य, कं त्वं वैश्वानरं वेत्थे- त्यप एव राजन्निति होवाचोमिति होवाचैष वै रयिर्वैश्वानर एतं हि वै त्वं रयिं वैश्वानरं वेत्थ नस्मात्तं [?] रयिमान्पुष्टिमानसि यो वा एतं रयिं पैश्वानरं [?] वेदाप पुनर्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायुरेति वस्ति स्ता एष वैश्वानरस्य वस्तिस्त्वाहास्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इति वस्तिस्तेऽविदितोऽभवि- ष्यद्यदि हनागमिष्य इति वा ॥५॥ (*atha hovāca satyayajñaṃ pauluṣiṃ | prācīnayogya, kaṃ tvaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vetthe- tyapa eva rājanniti hovācomiti hovācaiṣa vai rayirvaiśvānara etaṃ hi vai tvaṃ rayiṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vettha nasmāttaṃ [?] rayimānpuṣṭimānasi yo vā etaṃ rayiṃ paiśvānaraṃ [?] vedāpa punarmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyureti vasti stā eṣa vaiśvānarasya vastistvāhāsyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya iti vastiste 'vidito 'bhavi- ṣyadyadi hanāgamiṣya iti vā ‖5‖*)

*[The Sanskrit above, the next section of the story, is given here as supplied, without translation of the Kannada paraphrase, which is not in the supplied text; the window ends here with the number ‖ 5 ‖. The supplied text has a few doubtful words in it: नस्मात्तं (*nasmāttaṃ*) [?] and पैश्वानरं (*paiśvānaraṃ*) [?] are kept as supplied, marked [?].]*

*[Stray line in the supplied text: "[8] Vol. 14".]*

### Printed p. 114

*Running head: [114 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186]. The window continues the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa story in the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 of Sūkta 186 from p. 113; the Kannada paraphrase of section 5 comes first.*

Then, being asked, "O Prācīnayogya, whom do you know as Vaiśvānara?", Satyayajña Pauluṣi said, "Water only (*udaka*)". Approving this too, the king said, "That is the wealth of Vaiśvānara. Because you know the nature of that wealth, you are rich and of high standing. One who knows it conquers death and certainly obtains the full life. But this is only the inner treasury of Vaiśvānara. If you had not come here, your treasury too would have been useless, or would never have come into your view." ‖ 5 ‖

अथ होवाच महाशालं जाबालं । औपमन्यव कं त्वं वैश्वानरं वेत्थेत्या- काशमेव राजन्निति होवाचोमिति होवाचैष वै बहुलो वैशानर [?] एतं हि वै त्वं बहुलं वैश्वानरं वेत्थ तस्मा त्वं बहु प्रजया पशुभिरसि यो ना [?] एतं बहुलं वैश्वानरं वेदाप पुनर्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायु- रेत्यात्मा त्वा एष वैश्वानरस्यात्मा त्वाहास्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इत्यात्मा तेविदितोऽभविष्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इ तिवा ॥६॥ (*atha hovāca mahāśālaṃ jābālaṃ | aupamanyava kaṃ tvaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vetthetyā- kāśameva rājanniti hovācomiti hovācaiṣa vai bahulo vaiśānara [?] etaṃ hi vai tvaṃ bahulaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vettha tasmā tvaṃ bahu prajayā paśubhirasi yo nā [?] etaṃ bahulaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vedāpa punarmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyu- retyātmā tvā eṣa vaiśvānarasyātmā tvāhāsyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya ityātmā tevidito 'bhaviṣyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya i tivā ‖6‖*)

Then he asked Mahāśāla Jābāla: "O Aupamanyava, whom do you know as Vaiśvānara?" When he answered "Space (*ākāśa*)", approving that too he said: "That is the abundant (rich) Vaiśvānara; and because you know this you have abundance of cattle, sons and so on. One who knows this conquers death and obtains the full life. But this is only the main trunk of Vaiśvānara; if you had not come here, the chief principle of your body would have been useless, or would never have come to be known by you." ‖ 6 ‖

अथ होच बुडिलमाश्वतराश्विं । वैयाघ्रपद्य कं त्वं वैश्वानरं वेत्थेति वायुमेव राजन्निति होवाचोमिति होवाचैष वै पृथग्वर्त्मा. वैश्वानर एतु [?] हि वै त्वं पृथग्वर्त्मानं वैश्वानरं वेत्थ तस्मात्वां पृथग्रथश्रेणयोS- नुयांति यो वा एतं पृथग्वर्त्मानं वैश्वानरं वेदाप पुनर्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायुरेति प्राणस्त्वा विष [?] वैश्वानरस्य प्राणस्त्वाहास्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इति प्राणस्तेऽविदितोऽभविष्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इति वा ॥ ७ ॥ (*atha hoca buḍilamāśvatarāśviṃ | vaiyāghrapadya kaṃ tvaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vettheti vāyumeva rājanniti hovācomiti hovācaiṣa vai pṛthagvartmā. vaiśvānara etu [?] hi vai tvaṃ pṛthagvartmānaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vettha tasmātvāṃ pṛthagrathaśreṇayoS- nuyāṃti yo vā etaṃ pṛthagvartmānaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vedāpa punarmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyureti prāṇastvā viṣa [?] vaiśvānarasya prāṇastvāhāsyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya iti prāṇaste 'vidito 'bhaviṣyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya iti vā ‖ 7 ‖*)

Again he asked Buḍila Aśvatarāśvi: "O Vaiyāghrapadya, whom do you know as Vaiśvānara?" He said that Vāyu is Vaiśvānara. Accepting that too, the royal sage said: "Vāyu is the variety of ways of Vaiśvānara. Because you know this, chariots that travel by many ways follow you. One who knows this certainly conquers death and obtains the full life.

### Printed p. 115

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 115 (as supplied).*

But this is only the breath of Vaiśvānara. If you had not come to me, your breath too would have been useless, or you would have had no knowledge of it." ‖ 7 ‖

अथ होवाचेंद्रद्युम्नं भाल्लवेयं। वैयाघ्रपद्य कं त्वं वैश्वानरं वेत्थे- त्यादित्यमेव राजन्निति होवाचोमिति होवाचैष वै सुततेजा वैश्वानर एतं हि वै त्वां सुततेजसं वैश्वानरं वेत्थ तस्मात्तवैष सुतोऽद्यमानः पच्यमानोऽक्षीयमाणो गृहेषु तिष्ठति यो वा एतं सुततेजसं वैश्वा- नरं वेदाप पुनर्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायुरेति चक्षुस्त्वा एतद्वैश्वानरस्य चक्षुस्त्वाहास्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इति चक्षुस्तेऽविदितमभविष्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इति वा ॥८॥ (*atha hovāceṃdradyumnaṃ bhāllaveyaṃ| vaiyāghrapadya kaṃ tvaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vetthe- tyādityameva rājanniti hovācomiti hovācaiṣa vai sutatejā vaiśvānara etaṃ hi vai tvāṃ sutatejasaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vettha tasmāttavaiṣa suto 'dyamānaḥ pacyamāno 'kṣīyamāṇo gṛheṣu tiṣṭhati yo vā etaṃ sutatejasaṃ vaiśvā- naraṃ vedāpa punarmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyureti cakṣustvā etadvaiśvānarasya cakṣustvāhāsyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya iti cakṣuste 'viditamabhaviṣyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya iti vā ‖8‖*)

Then he asked Indradyumna Bhāllaveya, "Whom do you know as Vaiśvānara?" He said that Āditya is Vaiśvānara. Approving that too, he said: "Truly, the brilliance of Soma is Vaiśvānara. Because you know this, in your house soma-juice and its cooking are never wanting. One who knows this conquers death and obtains the full life. But this is only the eye of Vaiśvānara. If you had not come to me, your eye too would have been useless, or you would have had no knowledge of it." ‖ 8 ‖

अथ होवाच जनं शार्कराक्ष्यं । सायवस कं त्वं वैश्वानरं वेत्थेति दिवमेव राजन्निति होवाच ओमिति होवाचैष वाअतिष्ठा वैश्वानर एतं हि वॆ [?] त्वमतिष्ठा वैश्वानरं वेत्थ तस्मात्तं समानानतितिष्ठसि यो वा एतमतिष्ठां वैश्वानरं वेदाप पुनर्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायुरेति मूर्धा त्वा एष वैश्वानरस्य मूर्धा त्वाहास्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इति मूर्धा तेऽविदितोS- भविष्यद्यदि ह नागमिष्य इति वा ॥९॥ (*atha hovāca janaṃ śārkarākṣyaṃ | sāyavasa kaṃ tvaṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vettheti divameva rājanniti hovāca omiti hovācaiṣa vāatiṣṭhā vaiśvānara etaṃ hi vè [?] tvamatiṣṭhā vaiśvānaraṃ vettha tasmāttaṃ samānānatitiṣṭhasi yo vā etamatiṣṭhāṃ vaiśvānaraṃ vedāpa punarmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyureti mūrdhā tvā eṣa vaiśvānarasya mūrdhā tvāhāsyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya iti mūrdhā te 'viditoS- bhaviṣyadyadi ha nāgamiṣya iti vā ‖9‖*)

Afterwards he asked Jana Śārkarākṣya: "O Sāyavasa, whom do you know as Vaiśvānara?" He said it is the atmosphere (*antarikṣa*). Approving that too, he said: "The atmosphere is the most excellent part of Vaiśvānara. Because you know it you are the most excellent among all your equals. One who knows this conquers death and obtains the full life. But this is only the head of Vaiśvānara. If you had not come to me, your head too would have been useless, or you would have had no understanding of it." ‖ 9 ‖

तान् होवाच एते वै यूयं पृथग्वैश्वानरान्विद्वांसः पृथगन्नमघस्तं प्रादेशमात्रमिव ह वै देवाः सुविदिता अभिसंपन्नास्तथा तु व एनान्वक्ष्यामि यथा प्रादेशमात्रमेवाभिसंपादयिष्यामीति ॥१०॥ (*tān hovāca ete vai yūyaṃ pṛthagvaiśvānarānvidvāṃsaḥ pṛthagannamaghastaṃ prādeśamātramiva ha vai devāḥ suviditā abhisaṃpannāstathā tu va enānvakṣyāmi yathā prādeśamātramevābhisaṃpādayiṣyāmīti ‖10‖*)

### Printed p. 116

*Running head: [116 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186].*

At the end, addressing them all together, he said: "Each one of you has grasped only separate parts of Vaiśvānara; therefore you eat separate food. These well-known gods are (each) only of the measure of a span (*prādeśa*, "measure of a span" as printed in English); I shall explain to you in such a way that these deities are indeed only of the measure of a span." ‖ 10 ‖

स होवाच । मूर्धानमुपदिशन्नेष वा अतिष्ठा वैशानर [?] इति, चक्षुषी उप- दिशन्नु वाचैष वै सुततेजा वैश्वानर इति, नासिकेऽउपदिशन्नु वाचैष वै पृथ- ग्वर्त्मा वैश्वानर इति, मुख्यमाकाशमुपदिशन्नु वाचैष वै बहुलो वैश्वानर इति मुख्या अप उपदिशन्नु वाचैष वै रयिवैश्वानर इति ऋुबकमुपदिशन्नु- [?] वाचैष वै प्रतिष्ठा वैश्वानर इति, स एषोऽग्निर्वैश्वानरो यत्पुरुषः स यो हैतमेममग्निं [?] वैश्वानरं पुरुषविधं पुरुषेऽंतः प्रतिष्ठितं वेदाप पुनर्मृत्युं जयति सर्वमायुरेति न हास्यब्रुवाणं च न वैश्वानरो हिनस्ति ॥११॥ (*sa hovāca | mūrdhānamupadiśanneṣa vā atiṣṭhā vaiśānara [?] iti, cakṣuṣī upa- diśannu vācaiṣa vai sutatejā vaiśvānara iti, nāsike 'upadiśannu vācaiṣa vai pṛtha- gvartmā vaiśvānara iti, mukhyamākāśamupadiśannu vācaiṣa vai bahulo vaiśvānara iti mukhyā apa upadiśannu vācaiṣa vai rayivaiśvānara iti ṛubakamupadiśannu- [?] vācaiṣa vai pratiṣṭhā vaiśvānara iti, sa eṣo 'gnirvaiśvānaro yatpuruṣaḥ sa yo haitamemamagniṃ [?] vaiśvānaraṃ puruṣavidhaṃ puruṣe 'ṃtaḥ pratiṣṭhitaṃ vedāpa punarmṛtyuṃ jayati sarvamāyureti na hāsyabruvāṇaṃ ca na vaiśvānaro hinasti ‖11‖*)

At the end, pointing to the head, he said that this is the most excellent part of Vaiśvānara; showing the eyes, that this is the part of Vaiśvānara which is the brilliance of Soma; pointing towards the nose, that this is the variety of the ways of Vaiśvānara; indicating the space (the mouth), that this is the abundant Vaiśvānara; showing the principal water, that this is the wealth of Vaiśvānara; and showing the chin (beard), that this is the support of Vaiśvānara. He explained that this Vaiśvānara Agni is none other than the best of men, and is of the form of man and abides within man; that one who knows this Vaiśvānara Agni conquers death and obtains the full life; and that no harm will ever come to one who always praises him. ‖ 11 ‖

(श. ब्रा. १०, ६-१-१रिंद ११) (*(śa. brā. 10, 6-1-1riṃda 11)*)

अभिपित्वे—ई (*abhipitve—ī*) — for this word Sāyaṇa has given the explanation अभिपतनीये (*abhipatanīye*) ("to be approached"), "that is going on now, or that has now been reached". Yāska in the Nirukta ((नि ३-१५) (*(ni 3-15)*)) says अभिपित्वं अभिप्राप्तिं (*abhipitvaṃ abhiprāptiṃ*) and gives the very sense "that which is going on now, or that which is being done now".

विश्वं जगत्—ई (*viśvaṃ jagat—ī*) — for these words Sāyaṇa has given two kinds of meaning: एतत्सा वरस्याप्युलपक्षणं [?] । कृत्स्नं प्रपंचं (*etatsā varasyāpyulapakṣaṇaṃ [?] | kṛtsnaṃ prapaṃcaṃ*) — "all the world that does not move" is one kind of meaning; and यद्वा जगच्छब्दो यजमानरूपमनुष्यवाची । सर्वानपि यजमानान् (*yadvā jagacchabdo yajamānarūpamanuṣyavācī | sarvānapi yajamānān*) ("or the word *jagat* denotes men in the form of sacrificers; all the sacrificers") — the word *jagat* also indicates "men in the form of sacrificers". Therefore here *viśvaṃ jagat* means "all the sacrificers": thus he has given the second meaning. (The supplied text has वरस्याप्युलपक्षणं (*varasyāpyulapakṣaṇaṃ*) [?] and "ಯಜಮಾರನನ್ನೂ" [?], kept as supplied, marked [?].)

### Printed p. 117

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 117 (as supplied, "ಅ ೫"). The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 of Sūkta 186 from p. 116.*

मनीषा—मनीषया (*manīṣā—manīṣayā*) ("*manīṣā* = *manīṣayā*, by the lordship of the mind; that is, by one's own wish"): as his mind has told him, or as it has approved, that is, with the pleasure of the self: this is the sense.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

*[As supplied, the label line reads व्याकरणक्रिया (*vyākaraṇakriyā*) (without the usual label form); standard label inserted.]*

इळाभिः—ईड (*iḷābhiḥ—īḍa*) (root *īḍ*, "to praise"): shortening by the Vedic rule; by क्विप् च ऎंबुदरिंद (*kvip ca èṃbudariṃda*) the suffix *kvip*; the accent of the root remains; by टापंचापि हलंतानां यथा वाचा दिशा निशा (*ṭāpaṃcāpi halaṃtānāṃ yathā vācā diśā niśā*) ((कौ (*(kau*) २-४-८२) (*2-4-82)*), as printed) the feminine suffix *ṭāp* comes even after a consonant-ending word; instrumental plural form. सुशस्ति—शंसु (*suśasti—śaṃsu*) (root सुशस्ति—शंसु (*suśasti—śaṃsu*) "to praise"): by स्त्रीयां क्तिन् ऎंबुदरिंद क्तिन् (*strīyāṃ ktin èṃbudariṃda ktin*) the suffix *ktin*; by अनुदा- त्तोपदेशवनति—(पा. सू. ६-४-३७) (*anudā- ttopadeśavanati—(pā. sū. 6-4-37)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-37) loss of the nasal; "those in which the praises are good"; by नञ्सुभ्याम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१७२) (*nañsubhyām (pā. sū. 6-2-172)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-172) the last syllable is acute; by सुपां सुलुक् (पा. सू. ७-१-३९) (*supāṃ suluk (pā. sū. 7-1-39)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-39) *luk* of the ending when an instrumental plural follows. विश्वानरः—नरे (*viśvānaraḥ—nare*) ("*nare saṃjñāyām*", Pā. Sū. 6-3-129): since the word *nara* follows, the preceding word *viśva* is lengthened. एतु—इण् (*etu—iṇ*) (root *iṇ*, "to go"): imperative, third person singular; the verb-*nighāta* accent arises. मत्सथ—मदी (*matsatha—madī*) (root *madī*, in the sense of delight and anointing): the causal sense is implied; present tense, second person plural, the suffix *tha*; by the force of the word *bahulam* in सिब्बहुलं लेटि (पा. सू. ३-१-३४) (*sibbahulaṃ leṭi (pā. sū. 3-1-34)*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-34) *sip* here also; by खरि च ऎंबुदरिंद (*khari ca èṃbudariṃda*) the substitution of the voiceless letter for the *d* of the root; because *yathā* precedes there is no *nighāta*; by तास्यनुदात्तेत् (पा. सू ६-१-१८६) (*tāsyanudāttet (pā. sū 6-1-186)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-186) the *laḥ sārvadhātuka* is unaccented and so the accent of the root remains. मनीषा—सुपां (*manīṣā—supāṃ*) — by the sūtra मनीषा—सुपां सुलुक् (*manīṣā—supāṃ suluk*) the *luk* of the ending when an instrumental singular follows.

#### Ṛk 2

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads संधि तासाठः (*saṃdhi tāsāṭhaḥ*); standard label inserted. The Saṃhitā lines were broken in the supplied text and have been joined; two readings in it are doubtful and are marked [?].]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ नो॒ विश्व॒ आस्का॑ [?] गमंतु दे॒वा मि॒त्रो अ॑र्य॒मा वरु॑णः स॒जोषाः॑ ।
> भुव॒न्यथा॑ नो॒ विश्वे॑ वृ॒धासः॒ कर॑न्तु॒षाहा॑ [?] विधु॒रं न शवः॑ ॥ २ ॥

> *a no viśva āskā [?] gamaṃtu devā mitro aryamā varuṇaḥ sajoṣāḥ |*
> *bhuvanyathā no viśve vṛdhāsaḥ karantuṣāhā [?] vidhuraṃ na śavaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

### Printed p. 118

*Running head: [118 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 186].*

*[A stray line in the supplied text before the Pada: १ हदिनाल्कु १ (*1 hadinālku 1*); not part of the printed Pada.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> आ । नः । विश्वे । आस्क्र्याः [?] । गमंतु । देवाः । मित्रः । अर्यमा । वरुणः । सजोषाः ।
> भुवन् । यथा । नः । विश्वे । वृधासः । करन् । सुसहा [?] । विधुरं । न । शवः ॥ २ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> वैश्वदेवे पशावा नो विश्व इत्येषा हविषः पुरोनुवाक्या । आ नो देवाना-
> मुपवेतु शंस आ नो विश्व आस्त्वा [?] गमंतु देवाः । आ ३-७ । इति ॥
> नोऽस्माकं यज्ञं विश्वे सर्वॆ देवा आस्माः शत्रूणामास्कंदयितार आक्र-
> मितारो वा आ गमंतु । आगच्छंतु । के ते । मित्रोऽर्यमा वरुणश्च त्रयोऽपि
> सजोषाः समानप्रीतयः संत आगच्छंतु । किंतु विश्वे देवा नोस्माकं वृधासो
> वर्धयितारो यथा भुवन् भवेयुः तथा सुषाहा सुष्टु शत्रूणामभिभवेन नोs-
> स्मदीयं शपोऽन्नं विथुरं हीनं यथा न करन् न कुर्युः तथागच्छंतु ॥

*Gist:* May all the gods, with Mitra, Aryaman and Varuṇa united in affection, come to our sacrifice, overcoming foes, and not leave our food diminished.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

विश्वे (देवाः) (*viśve (devāḥ)*) — the Viśve Devāḥ. नः (*naḥ*) — to us. वृधासः (*vṛdhāsaḥ*) — as increasers. भुवन् (*bhuvan*) — as they became (and). सुषाहा (*suṣāhā*) — by defeating and destroying the enemies. नः (*naḥ*) — of us. शवः (*śavaḥ*) — the food. विधुरं (*vidhuraṃ*) — diminished. न करन् (*na karan*) — as they do not make (so). मित्रः (*mitraḥ*) — Mitra. आर्यमा (*āryamā*) — Aryaman (printed "ಆರಮ್ಯ"). वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*) — Varuṇa. विश्वे देवाः (*viśve devāḥ*) — all these gods. सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*) — of one equal affection. आस्क्राः (*āskrāḥ*) — resisting (our enemies). नः (*naḥ*) — to our sacrifice. आ गमंतु (*ā gamaṃtu*) — may they come.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May the Viśve Devāḥ become increasers for us, and, by defeating and destroying our enemies, not make our food nothing but augment it; and may Mitra, Aryaman (printed "ಅರಮ್ಯ") and Varuṇa, and all these gods too, united in equal affection and resisting our enemies, come to our sacrifice.

**Printed English translation**

May all the triumphant gods, Mitra, Aryaman, Varuna equally well pleased, come (to our rite) : may they all be propitious to us: may they not leave us in want of food, after overcoming (our foes).

### Printed p. 119

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 119 (as supplied, "ಅ. ೨ ಅ. ೫. ವ. ೪").*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

When the oblation addressed to the Viśve Devāḥ is offered in the animal sacrifice, this ṛk beginning आ नो विश्वे (*ā no viśve*) is to be used as the *puro-'nuvākyā* mantra; this is set out in the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra नो देवानामुपवेतु शंस आ नो विश्व आस्प्रागमंतु [?] देवाः ऎंब सूत्रदिंद।(आ. (*no devānāmupavetu śaṃsa ā no viśva āsprāgamaṃtu [?] devāḥ èṃba sūtradiṃda|(ā.*) (as printed).

आस्क्रा:-शत्रुगळन्नु (*āskrā:-śatrugaḷannu*) — those who defeat or attack the enemies. It is prayed that the gods, having defeated our enemies, come to our sacrificial ground and bestow on us food and other nourishment.

विश्वे देवाः—ऎल्ला देवतॆगळू (*viśve devāḥ—èllā devatègaḷū*) — all the gods are here prayed to come together with the deities Mitra, Aryaman and Varuṇa. If it is asked whether, when "all the gods" is said, the deities Mitra, Aryaman and Varuṇa are not already included among them, the answer is: although they are included, the names of these three are mentioned separately in order to stress that these three must come without fail.

मित्र: अर्यमा, वरुण:—ई मूरुदेवतॆगळू प्रत्येकदेवतॆगळॆंदु भाविसल्पट्टिद्दरू (*mitra: aryamā, varuṇa:—ī mūrudevatègaḷū pratyekadevatègaḷèṃdu bhāvisalpaṭṭiddarū*) — although these three deities are regarded as separate deities, it appears from what is explained here that they are the part-deities of that name among the many forms of the Sun, that is, among the twelve Ādityas. Besides, in some places it is said that these three deities are other forms of Agni:

त्वमग्ने वरुणो जायसे यत्त्वं मित्रो भवसि यत्समिद्धः । (*tvamagne varuṇo jāyase yattvaṃ mitro bhavasi yatsamiddhaḥ |*)
(((पु. सं ५-३-१) (*((pu. saṃ 5-3-1)*), as printed with a doubled parenthesis; the book prints "ಪು. ಸಂ")
त्वमर्यमा भवसि यत्कनीनां नाम स्वधावन् गुह्यं बिभर्षि । (*tvamaryamā bhavasi yatkanīnāṃ nāma svadhāvan guhyaṃ bibharṣi |*)
((मि. सं. ५-३-२) (*(mi. saṃ. 5-3-2)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮಿ. ಸಂ.")

in these ṛks it is said that Agni has these three names: Varuṇa when he is born, Mitra when he is well kindled and blazing, and Aryaman in the offerings connected with the maiden (marriage rites). Moreover, in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa:

मित्रोऽसि वरुणोऽसीत्याह । मैत्रं वा अहः । वारुणी रात्रिः । अहोरात्रा- भ्यामेवैनमुपावहरति (*mitro 'si varuṇo 'sītyāha | maitraṃ vā ahaḥ | vāruṇī rātriḥ | ahorātrā- bhyāmevainamupāvaharati*)
((कै. ब्रा, १-७-१०-१) (*(kai. brā, 1-7-10-1)*), as printed; the book prints "ಕೈ. ಬ್ರಾ,")

it is said that Mitra is the lord of the day and Varuṇa the lord of the night. It should therefore be noted that these three deities are in some places said to be part-deities of the Sun and in some places different names of Agni.

### Printed p. 120

*Running head: [120 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186]. The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 2 of Sūkta 186 (Mitra, Aryaman, Varuṇa) from p. 119; no new ṛk begins in this window.*

In the Purāṇas and elsewhere Mitra is one of the twelve Ādityas. In the Ṛgveda, though Mitra is another name of the Sun, Mitra has a distinct individuality, that is, a personal importance of his own. In the Ṛgveda Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman are said to be sons of Aditi, and so they are in many places called Ādityas. That these Ādityas, that is, the sons of Aditi, are six in number:

इमा गिर आदित्येभ्यो धृतस्नूः सनाद्राजभ्यो जुह्वा जुहोमि । (*imā gira ādityebhyo dhṛtasnūḥ sanādrājabhyo juhvā juhomi |*)
शृणोतु मित्रो अर्यमा भगो नस्तुविजातो वरुणो दक्षो अंशः ॥ (*śṛṇotu mitro aryamā bhago nastuvijāto varuṇo dakṣo aṃśaḥ ‖*)

((म सं. २-२७-१) (*(ma saṃ. 2-27-1)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ ಸಂ.")

that is, only six names are given: Mitra, Aryaman, Bhaga, Varuṇa, Dakṣa and Aṃśa.

देवा आदित्या ये सप्त तेभिः सोमाभि रक्ष न इंद्रायेंदो परि स्रव । (*devā ādityā ye sapta tebhiḥ somābhi rakṣa na iṃdrāyeṃdo pari srava |*)
((पु. सं ९-११४-३) (*(pu. saṃ 9-114-3)*), as printed; the book prints "ಪು. ಸಂ")

in this मक्किनल्लि (*makkinalli*) [?] [as printed] it is said that the sons of Aditi are seven.

अष्टौ पुत्रासो अदितेर्ये जातास्तन्व १ स्परि [?] । (*aṣṭau putrāso aditerye jātāstanva 1 spari [?] |*)
देवा उपप्रैत्सप्तभिः परा मार्तंडमास्यत् । (*devā upapraitsaptabhiḥ parā mārtaṃḍamāsyat |*)
सप्तभिः पुत्रैरदितिरुप प्रैत्तूर्व्यं युगं । (*saptabhiḥ putrairaditirupa praittūrvyaṃ yugaṃ |*)
प्रजायै मृत्यवे त्वत्पुनर्मार्तंडमाभरत् ॥ (*prajāyai mṛtyave tvatpunarmārtaṃḍamābharat ‖*)
((म. सं. १०-७२-८, ९) (*(ma. saṃ. 10-72-8, 9)*))

in these ṛks it is said that Aditi had first seven and then Mārtaṇḍa, eight sons in all. (The supplied text has जातास्तन्व १ स्परि [?] (*jātāstanva 1 spari [?]*) in the first line, with a stray "೧", marked [?].) Besides, in the Taittirīya Āraṇyaka:

अष्टा पुत्रासो आदितेः । ये जातास्तन्वः परि । देवा उपप्रैत्सप्तभिः । परामार्तंडमास्यत् । सप्तभिः पुत्रैरदितिः । उपप्रैत्सूर्व्यं युगं । प्रजायै मृत्यवे तत् । परामार्तंडमाभरदिति । ताननुक्रमिष्यामः । मित्रश्च वरुणश्च । धाता चार्यमा च । अगंशश्च भगश्च । इंद्रश्च विवस्वाग्- श्चेत्येते । (*aṣṭā putrāso āditeḥ | ye jātāstanvaḥ pari | devā upapraitsaptabhiḥ | parāmārtaṃḍamāsyat | saptabhiḥ putrairaditiḥ | upapraitsūrvyaṃ yugaṃ | prajāyai mṛtyave tat | parāmārtaṃḍamābharaditi | tānanukramiṣyāmaḥ | mitraśca varuṇaśca | dhātā cāryamā ca | agaṃśaśca bhagaśca | iṃdraśca vivasvāg- ścetyete |*)
((कृ. आ. १-१३-३) (*(kṛ. ā. 1-13-3)*), "ಕೃ. ಆ." as printed)

the names of the eight sons of Aditi are also given: Mitra, Varuṇa, Dhātā, Aryaman, Aṃśa, Bhaga, Indra and Vivasvān are said to be those eight. The same matter is stated in the Taittirīya Brāhmaṇa:

अदिति पुत्रकामा । साध्येभ्यो देवेभ्यो ब्रह्मौदनमपचत् । तस्या उच्चे- षणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्यै धाता चार्यमा चाजा- (*aditi putrakāmā | sādhyebhyo devebhyo brahmaudanamapacat | tasyā ucce- ṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyai dhātā cāryamā cājā-*)

### Printed p. 121

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 121 (as supplied).*

येतां । सा द्वितीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोधत्त । तस्यॆ मित्रश्च वरुणश्चाजायेतां । सा तृतीयमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोऽधत्त । तस्या अगंशश्च भगश्चाजायेतां । सा चतुर्थमपचत् । तस्या उच्छेषणमददुः । तत्पाश्नात् । सा रेतोऽधत्त । तस्या इंद्रश्च विवस्वाग्ंश्चाजायेतां (*yetāṃ | sā dvitīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā retodhatta | tasyè mitraśca varuṇaścājāyetāṃ | sā tṛtīyamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā reto 'dhatta | tasyā agaṃśaśca bhagaścājāyetāṃ | sā caturthamapacat | tasyā uccheṣaṇamadaduḥ | tatpāśnāt | sā reto 'dhatta | tasyā iṃdraśca vivasvāgṃścājāyetāṃ*)
((तै. ब्रा. १-१-९-१, २, ३.) (*(tai. brā. 1-1-9-1, 2, 3.)*))

The matter that Aditi obtained eight sons is clearly set out here. Of these eight, the three Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman are especially famous in the Ṛgveda. In the sūkta २-२७ने (*2-27ne*) of the Ṛg. Saṃ. there is a detailed description of the beneficent heroic deeds of Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman.

मित्रः—मित्रशब्दद (*mitraḥ—mitraśabdada*) — Yāska in his Nirukta has stated the derivation of the word *mitra* in this way:

मित्रः प्रमीतेस्त्रायते । संमिन्वानो द्रवतीति वा । मेदयतेर्वा । (*mitraḥ pramītestrāyate | saṃminvāno dravatīti vā | medayatervā |*)
((नि. १०-२१) (*(ni. 10-21)*))

What is the meaning of the word *mitra*? ऎंदरॆ—मित्रः कस्मात् । स हि प्रमीतेः त्रायते । प्रमीतिः मरणं । (*èṃdarè—mitraḥ kasmāt | sa hi pramīteḥ trāyate | pramītiḥ maraṇaṃ |*) — *pramīti* means death. Because this deity gives rain to the people in the world, and thereby provides food and so on, and saves and protects them from the fear of death that may come from hunger, he is called Mitra.

समंततो मिन्वानः उदकेन द्रवति अंतरिक्षलोके । (*samaṃtato minvānaḥ udakena dravati aṃtarikṣaloke |*) — because this deity, being lord of water and staying in the atmosphere, makes, through the clouds, all around, that is, the whole region, drenched, that is, wet with water, he is called Mitra. Or: अथवा-स्नेहनार्थस्य (*athavā-snehanārthasya*) [?] सर्वं ह्यसावुदकेन स्नेहयति । (*sarvaṃ hyasāvudakena snehayati |*) — because this deity makes everything cling together with water, that is, join together in one, he is called Mitra. Having given the derivation in three ways, in order to support it he has quoted the ṛk

मित्रो जनान् यातयति ब्रुवाणो मित्रो दाधार पृथिवीमुत द्यां । (*mitro janān yātayati bruvāṇo mitro dādhāra pṛthivīmuta dyāṃ |*)
मित्रः कृष्टीरनिमिषाभि चष्टे मित्राय हव्यं धृतवज्जु होत ॥ (*mitraḥ kṛṣṭīranimiṣābhi caṣṭe mitrāya havyaṃ dhṛtavajju hota ‖*)
((ऋ. सं. ३-५९-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 3-59-1)*))

as an example. In this ṛk Mitra, helping agricultural and other works, pouring rain and so on, providing people with food and the like, supports, that is, nourishes, the people of the earth and the dwellers of heaven. "O men, offer to this Mitra the deity

### Printed p. 122

*Running head: [122 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186].*

oblations and the like mixed with ghee": thus the unique qualities of Mitra and his beneficence to the world are very well described. The only sūkta in the Ṛgveda addressed to Mitra is this sūkta of 9 ṛks ((ऋ सं. ३-५९) (*(ṛ saṃ. 3-59)*)); elsewhere Mitra is praised together with Varuṇa and other deities.

Since the association of Mitra and Varuṇa is very close, the sūkta with Mitra alone as deity is, as said before, only one in the Ṛgveda ((ऋ. सं. ३-५९) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 3-59)*)); even there the praise of the deity is unclear. But in the first ṛk only the distinctive quality of Mitra is well stated. In the fifth ṛk of the same sūkta:

महा आदित्यो नमसोपसद्यो यातयज्जनो गृणते सुशेवः । (*mahā ādityo namasopasadyo yātayajjano gṛṇate suśevaḥ |*)
((म. सं. ३-५९-५) (*(ma. saṃ. 3-59-5)*))

this Mitra is called the great Āditya who gathers all men together. The word यातयज्ञन (*yātayajñana*) ("one who brings the people together") which gives this meaning,

व्रतेन स्थो ध्रुवक्षेमा धर्मणा यातॆयज्जना । (*vratena stho dhruvakṣemā dharmaṇā yātèyajjanā |*)
((ऋ. सं. ५-७२-२) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 5-72-2)*))

in this ṛk is applied to the pair Mitra and Varuṇa, and

मित्रस्तयोर्वरुणो यातयज्जनोऽर्यमा यातयज्जनः । (*mitrastayorvaruṇo yātayajjano 'ryamā yātayajjanaḥ |*)
((म. सं. १-१३६-३) (*(ma. saṃ. 1-136-3)*))

in this ṛk to the three, Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman. According to the sūkta ऋ. सं ३-५९ नॆय (*ṛ. saṃ 3-59 nèya*) [as above], Mitra is the support of heaven and earth; the five tribes of men (*pañcajanāḥ*) are obedient to him; he is the support of the life of all the other gods: so the qualities of Mitra are described. In ऋ सं. ५-८१-४ रल्लि (*ṛ saṃ. 5-81-4 ralli*) Savitṛ and Mitra are said to be one; in the Vājasaneyi Saṃhitā ((वा. सं. ४-३ ) (*(vā. saṃ. 4-3 )*)) Viṣṇu is said to have taken three steps following the rules of Mitra; in ऋ. सं. १०-८-४ रल्लि (*ṛ. saṃ. 10-8-4 ralli*) Agni, established before the dawn, is said to produce Mitra for himself; in ऋ. सं. ३-५-४ रल्लि (*ṛ. saṃ. 3-5-4 ralli*) the blazing Agni is said to be Mitra; in ऋ सं. ५-३-१ रल्लि (*ṛ saṃ. 5-3-1 ralli*) it is said that Agni is Varuṇa when born and Mitra when kindled;

मित्रो अग्निर्भवति यत्समिद्दो मित्रो होता वरुणो जातवेदाः । (*mitro agnirbhavati yatsamiddo mitro hotā varuṇo jātavedāḥ |*)
मित्रो अध्वर्युरिषिरो दमूना मित्रः सिंधूनामुत पर्वतानां ॥ (*mitro adhvaryuriṣiro damūnā mitraḥ siṃdhūnāmuta parvatānāṃ ‖*)
((ऋ. सं. ३-५-४) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 3-5-4)*))

in this ṛk the well-kindled Agni is described as Mitra, who is the Hotṛ and the Adhvaryu in the sacrifice, and Varuṇa, and the Agni called Jātavedas, and the friend (benefactor) of rivers and mountains: so the qualities of Mitra are described.

### Printed p. 123

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 123 (as supplied, "ವ. .೪"). The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 2 of Sūkta 186 (Mitra and Varuṇa) from p. 122; no new ṛk begins in this window.*

वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*) — Yāska's derivation: वरुणो वृणोतीति सतः [?] (*varuṇo vṛṇotīti sataḥ [?]*) ((नि. १०-२) (*(ni. 10-2)*)): because he is covered by masses of cloud this deity is called Varuṇa.

Varuṇa is a most eminent deity, equal to Indra in power and the like. The number of mantras addressed to Varuṇa is very small. There are twelve sūktas in the Ṛgveda addressed to Varuṇa, but there are twenty-four sūktas that praise Mitra and Varuṇa together.

Varuṇa has a human body and human actions; but rather than his physical body and actions the description of his moral actions and deeds is more prominent. He has a face, eyes, arms, hands and feet; he drives a chariot. This is stated plainly in the ṛks

चित्रं देवानामुदगादनीकं चक्षुर्मित्रस्य वरुणस्याग्नेः । (*citraṃ devānāmudagādanīkaṃ cakṣurmitrasya varuṇasyāgneḥ |*)
(ऋ. सं. १-११५-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-115-1)*)
उदु त्यच्यक्षुर्महि मित्रयोरा एति प्रियं वरुणयोरदब्धं । (*udu tyacyakṣurmahi mitrayorā eti priyaṃ varuṇayoradabdhaṃ |*)
(ऋ. सं. ६-५१-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 6-51-1)*)
उद्वां चक्षुर्वरुण सुप्रतीकं देवयोरेति सूर्यस्ततन्वान् । (*udvāṃ cakṣurvaruṇa supratīkaṃ devayoreti sūryastatanvān |*)
(ऋ सं. ७-६१-१) (*(ṛ saṃ. 7-61-1)*)
चक्षुर्मित्रस्य वरुणस्य देवश्चर्मेव यः समविव्यक् तमांसि । (*cakṣurmitrasya varuṇasya devaścarmeva yaḥ samavivyak tamāṃsi |*)
(ऋ. सं. ७-६३-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 7-63-1)*)
नमो मित्रस्य वरुणस्य चक्षसे महो देवाय तदृतं सपर्यत । (*namo mitrasya varuṇasya cakṣase maho devāya tadṛtaṃ saparyata |*)
(ऋ. सं. १०-३७-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 10-37-1)*)

That the Sun is, as it were, his eye is found in the very first ṛk of the sūktas addressed to Mitra and Varuṇa, so it seems that this is the aspect that first comes into view whenever Mitra and Varuṇa are mentioned. And in the ṛk ७-६६-१० (*7-66-10*) it is said that Aryaman, Mitra and Varuṇa have the Sun as eye.
