That Varuṇa is far-seeing (चक्षसं (*cakṣasaṃ*)) is said in the ṛks १-२५-५ (*1-25-5*), १-२५-१६ (*1-25-16*) and others.

आ नो बर्हि रिशादसो वरुणो मित्रो अर्यमा। सीदंतु मनुषो यथा॥ (*ā no barhi riśādaso varuṇo mitro aryamā| sīdaṃtu manuṣo yathā‖*)
(ऋ. सं. १-२६-४) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-26-4)*)
प्रतेन स्थो ध्रुवक्षेमा धर्मणा यातॆयज्जना । नि बर्हिषि सदतं सोम- (*pratena stho dhruvakṣemā dharmaṇā yātèyajjanā | ni barhiṣi sadataṃ soma-*)
पीतये ॥ (*pītaye ‖*)
(म. सं. ५-२-२) (*(ma. saṃ. 5-2-2)*)

### Printed p. 124

*[Page break of the print; the supplied text prints the header as "124 ಸಾಯಿಣಭಾವ್ಯಸಹಿತಾ [ ಮಂ. ೧. ಅ. ೨೪. ಸೂ. ೧೮೬" in the middle of the text, with no page marker.]*

In these and similar ṛks Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman are prayed to come and sit on the sacred grass spread on the sacrificial altar for the drinking of Soma.

यदी सखाया सख्याय सोमैः सुतेभिः सुप्रयसा मादय्यते । (*yadī sakhāyā sakhyāya somaiḥ sutebhiḥ suprayasā mādayyate |*)
(म. सं. ४-४१-३) (*(ma. saṃ. 4-41-3)*)

in these and similar ṛks it is said that Varuṇa drinks Soma along with Indra and other gods.

बिभ्रद्दापिं हिरण्ययं वरुणो वस्त निर्णिजं । परि स्पशो नि षेदिरे । (*bibhraddāpiṃ hiraṇyayaṃ varuṇo vasta nirṇijaṃ | pari spaśo ni ṣedire |*)
(म सं- १-२५-१३) (*(ma saṃ- 1-25-13)*)

in this ṛk there is the description that Varuṇa wears a golden mantle or robe and a shining garment. Varuṇa's chariot is golden, bright like the Sun, and has whips that urge the horses. Mitra and Varuṇa mount their chariot in a very high region of the sky ((ऋ सं ५-६३-१) (*(ṛ saṃ 5-63-1)*)); there are such descriptions. The dwelling of Mitra and Varuṇa is golden and is in the upper world. And Varuṇa, sitting in his palace:

नि षसाद धृतव्रतो वरुणः पस्ता३ [?] स्वा । साम्राज्याय सुकृतुः । (*ni ṣasāda dhṛtavrato varuṇaḥ pastā3 [?] svā | sāmrājyāya sukṛtuḥ |*)
अतो विश्वान्यद्भुता चिकित्त्वा अभि पश्यति । कृतानि या च कर्त्वा ॥ (*ato viśvānyadbhutā cikittvā abhi paśyati | kṛtāni yā ca kartvā ‖*)
(ऋ. सं. १-२५-१०, ११) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-25-10, 11)*)

sees all that goes on in the world. The dwelling of Mitra and Varuṇa is very large, very high, firm, resting on a thousand pillars; and their house has a thousand doors (ऋ. सं. ७-८८-५ (*ṛ. saṃ. 7-88-5*)). The all-seeing Sun leaves his own dwelling and goes to the house of Mitra and Varuṇa to report the doings of men, and enters their beloved house; there are such descriptions too.

Varuṇa, alone or with Mitra, is, like other deities, called king in many places:

अबुध्ने राजा वरुणो वनस्योर्ध्वं स्तूपं ददते पूतदक्षः । (*abudhne rājā varuṇo vanasyordhvaṃ stūpaṃ dadate pūtadakṣaḥ |*)
(ऋ. सं. १-२४-७) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-24-7)*)
त्वं विश्वेषां वरुणासि राजा ये च देवा असुर ये च मर्ताः । (*tvaṃ viśveṣāṃ varuṇāsi rājā ye ca devā asura ye ca martāḥ |*)
(ऋ. सं. २-२७-१०) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 2-27-10)*)
तेन विश्वस्य भुवनस्य राजा यवं न वृष्टिर्व्युनत्ति भूम । (*tena viśvasya bhuvanasya rājā yavaṃ na vṛṣṭirvyunatti bhūma |*)
(ऋ सं. ५-८५-३) (*(ṛ saṃ. 5-85-3)*)

### Printed p. 125

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 125 (as supplied).*

in these and similar ṛks it is said that Varuṇa is king not only of men but of the gods and of all the worlds. Besides Varuṇa, deities such as Indra, Yama and Soma also have the honorific name "king".

There are many stories connected with Indra, but with Varuṇa it is quite the opposite; there is not a single story. Varuṇa is much praised as the ordainer of physical and moral laws (वरुणस्य व्रतानि (*varuṇasya vratāni*)). The divine power of Mitra and Varuṇa is described by the word माया (*māyā*). When applied to the gods it means the hidden power they have to do good; when applied to demons the same word means the hidden power they have to do evil. In the good sense this word suits Mitra and Varuṇa; in the bad sense it applies to demons. As with the word असुर (*asura*), the word *māyā* is used in both senses. By this hidden *māyā* power in himself Varuṇa, standing in the atmosphere, measures the earth by the Sun as with a measuring rod. He makes the Sun cross the atmosphere and makes him dim with rain clouds. Then sweet (honey-mixed) drops fall ((ऋ. सं. ५-६३-४) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 5-63-4)*)). He makes the sky pour rain. Therefore this word *māyā* is applied chiefly to Varuṇa among the gods in the ṛks ६-४८-१४ (*6-48-14*); ७-२८-४ (*7-28-4*); १०-९९-१० (*10-99-10*); १०-१४७-५ (*10-147-5*) and others.

Varuṇa is the one who lays down the laws of nature; it is he who established earth and heaven. And

आसीदद्विश्वा भुवनानि संराड्विश्वेत्तानि वरुणस्य व्रतानि । (*āsīdadviśvā bhuvanāni saṃrāḍviśvettāni varuṇasya vratāni |*)
(मि, सं. ८-४२-१) (*(mi, saṃ. 8-42-1)*)

in this ṛk it is said that Varuṇa dwells in all the worlds and that his laws are observed everywhere;

तिस्रो द्यावो निहिता अंतरस्मिन् तिस्रो भूमीरुःपराः षड्विधानाः । (*tisro dyāvo nihitā aṃtarasmin tisro bhūmīruḥparāḥ ṣaḍvidhānāḥ |*)
(ऋ. सं. ७-८७-५) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 7-87-5)*)

in this it is said that the three upper worlds and the three lower worlds (the nether worlds) are in his power. Varuṇa and Mitra rule the whole universe ((ऋ. सं. ५-६३-७) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 5-63-7)*)); they are the protectors of the whole world ((ऋ. सं. २-२७-४) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 2-27-4)*)); Varuṇa is the support of heaven, earth and sky ((ऋ. सं. ५-६९-१, ४) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 5-69-1, 4)*)). He has placed fire in the water, the Sun in the sky, Soma on the stone. He has made a wide path for the Sun. Varuṇa, Mitra and

### Printed p. 126

*Running head: [126 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24: Sū. 186] (the colon is as supplied).*

Aryaman clear a path for the Sun ((ऋ सं ७-६०-४) (*(ṛ saṃ 7-60-4)*)). In many such ṛks the authority of Varuṇa is described.

In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā ((तै सं. ६-४-८-३) (*(tai saṃ. 6-4-8-3)*)) मित्रोऽहरजनयद्वरुणो रात्रिं (*mitro 'harajanayadvaruṇo rātriṃ*) — that is, Mitra created the day and Varuṇa the night. Therefore मैत्रं वा अहः । (*maitraṃ vā ahaḥ |*) नारुणी [?] रात्रिः (*nāruṇī [?] rātriḥ*) [the print has "ನಾರುಣೀ" for "ವಾರುಣೀ"]: it is said in many places that Mitra is lord of the day and Varuṇa lord of the night ((तै सं. २-१-७-४) (*(tai saṃ. 2-1-7-4)*)). In one place it is said that Varuṇa divided the seasons.

वेद मासो धृतव्रतो द्वादश प्रजावशः । वेदा य उपजायते । (*veda māso dhṛtavrato dvādaśa prajāvaśaḥ | vedā ya upajāyate |*)
((म. सं. १-२५-८) (*(ma. saṃ. 1-25-8)*), as printed with the reference after the text)

here it is said that Varuṇa knows the twelve months, and

वि ये दधुः शरदं मासमादहर्यज्ञ मक्तुं चादृचं । (*vi ye dadhuḥ śaradaṃ māsamādaharyajña maktuṃ cādṛcaṃ |*)
अनाप्यं वरुणो मित्रो अर्यमा क्षत्रं राजान आशत ॥ (*anāpyaṃ varuṇo mitro aryamā kṣatraṃ rājāna āśata ‖*)
(म. सं ७-६६-११) (*(ma. saṃ 7-66-11)*)

in this ṛk it is said that Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman divided autumn, the month, the day and the night.

It is said in many places that it is he too who regulates the waters. It is he who made the rivers flow; by his command they flow ceaselessly ((ऋ. सं. २-२८-४) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 2-28-4)*)). That the rivers, though they rush into the sea and pour water, do not fill it, is by his hidden power ((ऋ. सं. ५-८५-६) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 5-85-6)*)). Varuṇa and Mitra are lords of the rivers. That Varuṇa is lord of the oceans is an established point, but it occurs very rarely in the Ṛgveda; perhaps the reason is that it is of no great importance. In the Purāṇas and the like Varuṇa is famous as lord of the waters, and it is said in the Purāṇas that, as one of the eight guardians of the quarters, he is lord of the west. There is no support for that in the Ṛgveda. In general all that is connected with Varuṇa is connected with the water in the atmosphere; among the deities prayed to for rain, Varuṇa and Mitra are chief. When the mountains are all covered by clouds, Varuṇa, with the overturned *bhāni* (cloud), pours water on heaven, earth and sky and wets the whole earth: so it is said in the ṛks ऋ. सं. ५-८५-३, ४नॆय (*ṛ. saṃ. 5-85-3, 4nèya*). A whole sūkta (ऋ. सं ५-६३नॆय (*ṛ. saṃ 5-63nèya*)) indicates their rain-giving power. In the Yajurveda Saṃhitā ((तै. सं ५-५ ४-१) (*(tai. saṃ 5-5 4-1)*)) आपो वरुणस्य पत्नय आसन् (*āpo varuṇasya patnaya āsan*) — the waters were Varuṇa's wives; and (तै. सं. ६-४-३-२) (*tai. saṃ. 6-4-3-2)*)) मित्रावरुणौ ना अपा नेतार् (*mitrāvaruṇau nā apā netār*) [?] ("Mitra and Varuṇa are the leaders of the waters"; the print has "ವಿತ್ರಾವರುಣರು" [?] in the Kannada) is also said.

### Printed p. 127

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 127 (as supplied). The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 2 of Sūkta 186 (Varuṇa, then Aryaman) from p. 126.*

It is said again and again that the decrees of Varuṇa are well established; for this very reason he is called धृतव्रत (*dhṛtavrata*) ("he of firm laws"). This epithet is given to both Mitra and Varuṇa.

परि धामानि मर्मृशद्वरुणस्य पुरो गये विश्वे देवा अनु व्रतं नभंता- (*pari dhāmāni marmṛśadvaruṇasya puro gaye viśve devā anu vrataṃ nabhaṃtā-*)
मन्यके समे ॥ (*manyake same ‖*)
((म. सं. ८-४१-७) (*(ma. saṃ. 8-41-7)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")
न वां देवा अमृता आ मिनंति व्रतानि मित्रावरुणा ध्रुवाणि । (*na vāṃ devā amṛtā ā minaṃti vratāni mitrāvaruṇā dhruvāṇi |*)
((म. सं. ५-६९-४) (*(ma. saṃ. 5-69-4)*))

in these and similar ṛks it is said that the gods themselves obey the commands of Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman, and that even the ancient gods cannot violate their firm laws. Mitra and Varuṇa are the regulators of law (*ṛta*) and light. It is by the strength of these decrees that they are able to support the laws. This quality is found especially in these two, sometimes in the Ādityas, or in other common deities too. The epithet ऋतस्य गोपाः (*ṛtasya gopāḥ*) ("guardians of *ṛta*"), that is, those who keep the laws (or make them be kept), is found applied many times to Mitra and Varuṇa.

The power of Varuṇa is unfathomable. Neither flying birds nor flowing rivers can reach the limit of his kingdom's border; they cannot exceed his power; they cannot withstand his anger. Neither the sky nor the rivers have reached the boundary of the territory of Mitra and Varuṇa. All things lie hidden in Varuṇa. The dwellings of all beings and the three heavens and the three earths are laid in him. Varuṇa is omniscient. The flight of birds in the sky, the course of ships on the sea, the path of the far-travelling wind: all these are known to him. He sees all things past or to come. He is the witness of the true and false deeds of men. All the deeds, thoughts and devices of every man are known to Varuṇa. He can grasp all that is in heaven and earth and beyond them. However far one runs beyond the sky, no one can leave the range of Varuṇa's sight.

Since he is the regulator of morals, Varuṇa becomes very angry at sin; for both the violation of his laws and his anger he inflicts very cruel punishment. The noose of Varuṇa that binds sinners is spoken of in the ṛks १-२४-१५ ; १-२५-११ ; ६-७४-४ १०-८५-२४ (*1-24-15 ; 1-25-11 ; 6-74-4 10-85-24*) and others; that noose binds the speaker of untruth and releases the speaker of truth.

युवो राष्ट्रं बृहदिन्वति द्यौर्यौ सेतृभिररज्जुभिः सिनीथः । (*yuvo rāṣṭraṃ bṛhadinvati dyauryau setṛbhirarajjubhiḥ sinīthaḥ |*)

### Printed p. 128

*Running head: [128 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 185] (the supplied text reads "೧೮೫" here for 186, as printed).*

परि नो हेळो [?] वरुणस्य वृज्या उरुं न इंद्रः कृणवदु लोकं ॥ (*pari no heḷo [?] varuṇasya vṛjyā uruṃ na iṃdraḥ kṛṇavadu lokaṃ ‖*)
(ऋ. सं. ७-८४-२) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 7-84-2)*)
(म. सं. ७-८४-२) (*(ma. saṃ. 7-84-2)*)

(the reference is printed twice, first as "ಋ. ಸಂ." and then as "ಮ. ಸಂ.")

in this ṛk it is said that Varuṇa, accompanied by Indra, binds with bonds that are not of rope. The word *pāśa* (noose) occurs in the whole Ṛgveda only in one place, in a mantra addressed to Agni, and that too in the context of praying that the noose that binds his worshippers be loosed; therefore it may be said that these acts of binding with the noose and of releasing it belong to Varuṇa alone.

Varuṇa together with Mitra is the one who drives away, hates and punishes untruth. Mitra and Varuṇa punish those who are careless in their worship, but Varuṇa forgives those who repent. He unties as one unties a rope, and removes sin; he frees a man not only from sin committed by himself but also from sin committed by his ancestors. Those who daily violate the laws laid down by him and afterwards repent he also saves, and he shows compassion to those who have violated his commands through forgetfulness. In Varuṇa there are hundreds and thousands of medicines. He drives away death and removes sin. He can take away life and can give life. He is very skilful among those who protect immortality. Thus the description of the qualities of Varuṇa is in many ṛks. Here we have explained it very briefly; on a fitting occasion we shall explain it at length.

In the Taittirīya Saṃhitā: फल्गुनीनक्षत्रमर्यमा देवता (*phalgunīnakṣatramaryamā devatā*) .... .... .... .... अनूराधा (*anūrādhā*) नक्षत्रं मित्रो देवता (*nakṣatraṃ mitro devatā*) .... .... .... .... ((तै. सं. ४-४-९-२)—ऎंदरॆ (*(tai. saṃ. 4-4-9-2)—èṃdarè*)) — that is, it is said that Aryaman is the lord of the Pūrva Phalgunī nakṣatra and Mitra of the Anurādhā nakṣatra.

आर्यमा—आदित्यर (*āryamā—ādityara*) — in the sūktas addressed to the Ādityas the Ādityas are generally Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman. As we said before, in the Ṛgveda ((ऋ. सं. २-२७-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 2-27-1)*)) only the names of six are given: Mitra, Aryaman, Bhaga, Varuṇa, Dakṣa and Aṃśa. In the ṛk ऋ सं. ९-११४-३ नॆय ऋक्किनल्लि (*ṛ saṃ. 9-114-3 nèya ṛkkinalli*) the number of the Ādityas is said to be seven, and in ऋ. सं. १०-७२-८ रल्लि (*ṛ. saṃ. 10-72-8 ralli*) it is said that Aditi first presented seven sons to the gods and afterwards obtained the eighth, Mārtaṇḍa. In अ. वे. ८-९-२१ (*a. ve. 8-9-21*) and in तै. ब्रा. (*tai. brā.*) १-१-९-१ (*1-1-9-1*) it is said that Aditi has eight sons: Mitra, Aryaman, Varuṇa, Aṃśa, Bhaga, Dhātṛ, Indra and Vivasvān. In the Śatapatha Brāhmaṇa ((श. ब्रा. ६-१-२.८ ; ११-६-३-८) (*(śa. brā. 6-1-2.8 ; 11-6-3-8)*)) it is said that the Ādityas are twelve, one for each month. In the Purāṇas and the like also the Ādityas are twelve.

Though the name of the deity Aryaman occurs about a hundred times in the Ṛgveda, because he has no complete individuality Yāska, in his Nirukta (the Nighaṇṭu), when explaining the forms and derivations of the names of deities, has left out the name of this deity Aryaman. Except in two mantras,

### Printed p. 129

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4. ।] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 129 (as supplied, with a danda).*

everywhere else he is mentioned together with other deities, and mostly with Mitra and Varuṇa. When addressing Agni in winning over maidens:

त्वमर्यमा भवसि यत्कनीनां नाम स्वधावन् गुह्यं बिभर्षि । (*tvamaryamā bhavasi yatkanīnāṃ nāma svadhāvan guhyaṃ bibharṣi |*)
(( ऋ. सं. ५-३-२) (*( ṛ. saṃ. 5-3-2)*))

he is said to be Aryaman: "you yourself."

भगो अर्यमा सविता पुरंधिर्मह्यं त्वादुर्गार्हपत्याय देवाः । (*bhago aryamā savitā puraṃdhirmahyaṃ tvādurgārhapatyāya devāḥ |*)
((ऋ. सं. १०-८५-३६) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 10-85-36)*))

in this ṛk the bridegroom says: "O gods, Bhaga, Aryaman, Savitṛ and Purandhi gave this bride to me to assist my householder's life."

न तमंहो न दुरितं देवासो अष्ट मर्त्यं । (*na tamaṃho na duritaṃ devāso aṣṭa martyaṃ |*)
सजोषसो यमर्यमा मित्रो नयंति वरुणो अति द्विषः ॥ (*sajoṣaso yamaryamā mitro nayaṃti varuṇo ati dviṣaḥ ‖*)
((म. सं. १०-१२६-१) (*(ma. saṃ. 10-126-1)*), as printed; the book prints "ಮ. ಸಂ.")
शुनमस्मभ्यमूतये वरुणो मित्रो आर्यमा । (*śunamasmabhyamūtaye varuṇo mitro āryamā |*)
शर्म यच्छंतु सप्रथ आदित्यासो यदीमहे अति द्विषः ॥ (*śarma yacchaṃtu sapratha ādityāso yadīmahe ati dviṣaḥ ‖*)
((म. सं. १०-१२६-७) (*(ma. saṃ. 10-126-7)*))

Although in the sūkta ऋ. सं. १०-१२६ (*ṛ. saṃ. 10-126*) the gods are called Viśve Devāḥ, from the ṛks quoted above it is chiefly the Ādityas who are praised here. Besides, the words देवासः अष्ट (*devāsaḥ aṣṭa*) ("eight gods") in the first ṛk refer to the eight Ādityas (the number of the Ādityas is nowhere said in the Veda to be more than eight); and the word आदित्यासः (*ādityāsaḥ*) in the seventh ṛk also makes this plain. That in every ṛk of this sūkta (except the last) the names वरुणो मित्रो अर्यमा (*varuṇo mitro aryamā*) ("Varuṇa, Mitra, Aryaman") are recited again and again like a refrain shows that among the Ādityas these Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman hold the chief place. Since not much about Aryaman is explained in the Ṛgveda, no special explanation is possible.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

गमंतु—गम्‌ल्य (*gamaṃtu—gamlya*) (root गमंतु—गम्‌ल्य (*gamaṃtu—gamlya*) "to go"): when the third person plural of the imperative follows, by बहुलं (*bahulaṃ*) छंदसि ऎंबुदरिंद (*chaṃdasi èṃbudariṃda*) the *luk* of the thematic suffix; because it follows a non-verb the *nighāta* accent arises. भुवन्—भू (*bhuvan—bhū*) (root भुवन्—भू (*bhuvan—bhū*) "to be"): when the optative follows, by लिज्याशिष्यज् (*lijyāśiṣyaj*) ((पा. सू. ३-१-८६) (*(pā. sū. 3-1-86)*)) the suffix *aj*; because it is marked *j* there is no guṇa of the *u* of the root; by अचिश्नु- (*aciśnu-*) धातुभ्रुवां (*dhātubhruvāṃ*) ... [the sentence breaks off at the end of p. 129 and continues in the next window]: आगमशास्त्रमनित्यम् (परि. ९३ २) (*āgamaśāstramanityam (pari. 93 2)*) (as printed).

*[Stray lines in the supplied text, twice: "[9] Vol. 14". The window ends mid-sentence.]*

### Printed p. 130

*Running head: [130 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 186], with tabs between the words in the supplied text. The window continues the Ṛk 2 grammar page of Sūkta 186 from p. 129, in the middle of the note on *bhuvan*.*

... [the sentence begins on p. 129, with the rule "ಆಗಮಶಾಸ್ತ್ರಮನಿತ್ಯಮ್ (ಪರಿ. ೯೩ ೨)"] the augment यासुटागम (*yāsuṭāgama*) (as printed) does not occur. Because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta* accent; by the accent of the root, the first syllable is acute. वृधासः—वृधु (*vṛdhāsaḥ—vṛdhu*) (root वृधासः—वृधु (*vṛdhāsaḥ—vṛdhu*) "to grow"): by इगुपधज्ञाप्रीकिरः कॆः ऎंबुदरिंद (*igupadhajñāprīkiraḥ kèḥ èṃbudariṃda*) the suffix *ka*; because it is marked *k* there is no guṇa of the penultimate short vowel; by the suffix accent the last syllable is acute; when *jas* follows it, by आज्जसेरसुक् (पा. सू- ७-१-५०) (*ājjaserasuk (pā. sū- 7-1-50)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-50) the augment *asuk*. करन्-डुकृञ् (*karan-ḍukṛñ*) (root करन्-डुकृञ् (*karan-ḍukṛñ*) "to do"): by छंदसि लुज्लज्‌लिटः ऎंबुदरिंद (*chaṃdasi lujlajliṭaḥ èṃbudariṃda*) *luṅ* in the sense of the imperfect (*laṅ*); by कैमृदुरुहिभ्यः-(पा. सू. ३-१-५९) (*kaimṛduruhibhyaḥ-(pā. sū. 3-1-59)*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-59) *aj* for *cli*; by गुणः (पा सू. ७-४-१६) (*guṇaḥ (pā sū. 7-4-16)*) (Pā. Sū. 7-4-16) guṇa with *r* of the *ṛ* of the root when *aj* follows; by बहुलं छंदस्यमाज्‌योगेऽपि (पा. सू. ६-४-७५) (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasyamājyoge 'pi (pā. sū. 6-4-75)*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-75) the augment *aṭ* does not occur; because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta* accent (the print has "ನಿಘಾಶಸ್ವರ" as supplied); the accent of the root remains. सुसाहा—सह (*susāhā—saha*) (root सुसाहा—सह (*susāhā—saha*) "to bear, to endure"): the suffix *kvip*; in a compound with a prefix the accent of the later member, a *kṛt* word; instrumental singular form (printed "ವಿಕವಚನ", as supplied); the lengthening in the Saṃhitā is Vedic. विथुरम्—व्यथॆ (*vithuram—vyathè*) (root विथुरम्—व्यथॆ (*vithuram—vyathè*) "to tremble, to fear"): by व्यथेः संप्रसारणं किच्चि (उ सू. १-३९) (*vyatheḥ saṃprasāraṇaṃ kicci (u sū. 1-39)*) (Uṇ. Sū. 1-39) the suffix *uraca*; since the prohibition न संप्रसारणे संप्रसारणम् (*na saṃprasāraṇe saṃprasāraṇam*) applies, *saṃprasāraṇa* of *y* to *i* occurs, the earlier form replaced by the later; because the suffix has the mark *c*, by चितः (पा. सू. ६-१-१६३) ऎंबुदरिंद (*citaḥ (pā. sū. 6-1-163) èṃbudariṃda*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-163) the last syllable is acute.

#### Ṛk 3

### Printed p. 131

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 131 (as supplied, "ವ ೪").*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे देवाः वो युष्माकं प्रेष्ठं प्रियतमं । सर्वेषामाह्वातृत्वाद्धविर्व-
> हनाच्चाग्निर्देवानां प्रियतमः । तमतिथिं गृणीषे । गृणे । सौमि ॥ व्यत्ययेन
> मध्यमः ॥ केन साधनेन । शस्तिभिः शंसनैः स्तुतिभिः । कीदृशोऽहं । तुर्वणिस्तूर्ण-
> वनिः शीघ्रं संभक्ता सजोषा युष्माभिः सह प्रीयमाणः । यद्वा । एते अग्नि विशे-
> षणे । यस्तुर्वणिः शीघ्रं यज्ञस्य संभक्ता सजोषा युष्माभिः सह प्रियमाणः
> तमग्निं गृणिषे । यथा येन प्रकारेण नोऽस्माकं वरुण एतन्नामको देवः
> सुकीर्तिः शोभनकीर्तिमानसत् । सुकीर्तिं भिक्षे वरुणस्य भूरेः । ऋ. २-२८-१ ।
> इत्यादिमंत्रवर्णेषु वरणात्सुकीर्तिरेवैषणीया । अरिगूर्तोऽरिष्टदातृषु सदा
> शब्दितः अरीणां हनना- योद्युक्तो वा सूरिः प्रेरयितायं वरुण इषश्चान्नानि
> पर्षत् । पूरयेत् । तथा तमपि स्तुम इत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* O gods, I praise Agni, your dearest guest, with praises, swift and in accord with you; so may Varuṇa be of fair renown to us, fill us with food, and so we praise him too.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

देवाः (*devāḥ*) — O gods. तुर्वणिः (*turvaṇiḥ*) — eager to perform the sacrifice and please the gods. सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*) — beloved equally by all the gods: I. [Or (यः (*yaḥ*) — whichever Agni), तुर्वणिः (*turvaṇiḥ*) — eager to carry the oblation to the gods, सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*) — equally beloved among the gods] who is such, and: वः (*vaḥ*) — to you. श्रेष्ठं— (*śreṣṭhaṃ—*) — the most beloved. अतिथिं (*atithiṃ*) — guest-like. अग्निं (*agniṃ*) — Agni. शस्तिभिः (*śastibhiḥ*) — with praises. गृणीषे (*gṛṇīṣe*) — I praise. यथा (*yathā*) — in what manner. अरिगूर्तः (*arigūrtaḥ*) — the destroyer of the enemies who give nothing. सूरिः (*sūriḥ*) — and the inspirer of men to action. वरुणः (*varuṇaḥ*) — Varuṇa. सुकीर्तिः (*sukīrtiḥ*) — of excellent fame. असत् (*asat*) — may he become, (and). इषश्च (*iṣaśca*) — food. पर्षत् (*parṣat*) — may he fill (in that way I praise Varuṇa also).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O gods, I, eager to perform the sacrifice and please the gods and equally beloved of all the gods, praise with hymns Agni, who is dearest to the gods and guest-like; and I praise Varuṇa too, the destroyer of those who give nothing and the inspirer of men to action, so that he may become for us a maker of excellent fame and fill us with food.

**Printed English translation**

I praise with hymns, (O gods), your best beloved guest, Agai [sic], who is prompt (to partake of the oblation), and who is well pleased along (with you); so that (thereby) Varuna, the possessor of renown, the subduer of foes, the animator (of men), may fill us with food.

### Printed p. 132

*Running head: [132 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186].*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads विशेषविवरगळु (*viśeṣavivaragaḷu*); standard label inserted.]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

The Viśve Devāḥ are said to be the deity of this sūkta. Therefore, having praised chiefly the deity Savitṛ in the first ṛk of this sūkta and the deities Mitra, Varuṇa and Aryaman (the Ādityas) in the second ṛk, the ṛṣi praises Agni and Varuṇa in the present third ṛk, that beginning प्रेष्ठं वो अतिथिं (*preṣṭhaṃ vo atithiṃ*).

प्रेष्ठं (*preṣṭhaṃ*) — प्रियतमं । सर्वेषामाह्वातृत्वादविर्वहनाच्चाग्निर्देवानां (*priyatamaṃ | sarveṣāmāhvātṛtvādavirvahanāccāgnirdevānāṃ*) प्रियतमः (*priyatamaḥ*) ("dearest; because he calls all and carries the oblations, Agni is dearest of the gods"): the meaning is "Agni, who is extremely beloved". How Agni is beloved of the gods: in the sacrifices and the like that men perform, Agni takes the office of Hotṛ, invites the gods intended by the sacrificers and brings them to the sacrificial ground, and he carries the oblations that the sacrificers offer in Agni to the gods and makes them reach them; therefore it is natural to say that Agni is beloved of the gods. In the sacrifices too Agni has a close bond with the daily life of men. Agni delivers to the gods what is offered by men in the fire; for this very reason he has the name *havyavāṭ* (हव्यवाट् (*havyavāṭ*), "carrier of oblation"). Without him the gods feel no delight. This is said in the ṛk

महा अस्यध्वरस्य प्रकेशो न ऋते त्वदमृता मादयंते । (*mahā asyadhvarasya prakeśo na ṛte tvadamṛtā mādayaṃte |*)
((ऋ सं ७-११-१) (*(ṛ saṃ 7-11-1)*), as printed with "ಋ ಸಂ")

And in the matter of Agni bringing the gods and carrying the oblation to them:

अग्निं दूतं वृणीमहे होतारं विश्ववेदसं । (*agniṃ dūtaṃ vṛṇīmahe hotāraṃ viśvavedasaṃ |*)
((म. सं. १-१२-१) (*(ma. saṃ. 1-12-1)*))

in this ṛk Agni is prayed to as the messenger who brings the gods to the sacrificial ground and summons them.

अग्ने देवा इहावह जज्ञानो व्यक्तबर्हिषे । असि होता न ईड्यः । (*agne devā ihāvaha jajñāno vyaktabarhiṣe | asi hotā na īḍyaḥ |*)
((ऋ. सं. १-१२-२) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-12-2)*))

in this ṛk Agni is prayed: "O Agni, since you are the Hotṛ who brings the gods, bring the gods to this sacrificial ground." In १-१४-१ (*1-14-1*) it is prayed देवेभिर्याहि (*devebhiryāhi*) ("come with the gods") to our sacrifice. Agni goes by both paths, to the earth and to the vicinity of the gods; because of that it is the custom to call him the messenger (*dūta*).

### Printed p. 133

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4.] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 133 (as supplied). The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 3 of Sūkta 186 from p. 132.*

Because it is said that in sacrifices Agni plays the most important part in accomplishing the rites, the position that men give to the priest is given to Agni among the gods. Hence he has epithets such as priest (*ṛtvij*), sage (*vipra*), family-priest (*purohita*) and *hotā*. Of all these, to call Agni *hotā* (one who invites the gods, or who accomplishes the oblation) is the most distinctive. He is the dearest and best of all the Hotṛs. The work of carrying the oblations is Agni's chief work. Therefore the ṛṣi describes Agni as most beloved of the gods.

अतिथिं (*atithiṃ*) — Since it is said that man obtains Agni by the grace of the gods and that Mātariśvan first saw Agni and brought him to earth, it is established, as it were, that Agni belongs to the world of heaven. It is in this sense that Agni may be called the guest of men. Or, since a guest is always honourable and worthy of respect, Agni too, being worthy of worship, may have been praised by the epithet "guest".

तुर्वणिः (*turvaṇiḥ*) — Yāska has stated the derivation of this word: तुर्वणिस्तूर्णवनिः (*turvaṇistūrṇavaniḥ*) ((नि. ६-१४) (*(ni. 6-14)*)). For this word, formed from the root वन षण संभक्तौ (*vana ṣaṇa saṃbhaktau*), the meaning is तूर्णं हि यः संभजते स तूर्णवनिरित्युक्तः (*tūrṇaṃ hi yaḥ saṃbhajate sa tūrṇavanirityuktaḥ*): "one who comes promptly". In the present ṛk तुर्वणिः सजोषाः (*turvaṇiḥ sajoṣāḥ*) means that Agni is praised as one who, loved by the other gods or accompanied by them, comes quickly to this our sacrificial ground.

सजोषाः (*sajoṣāḥ*) — the bhāṣyakāra has given two kinds of meaning for this word. First, सजोषा युष्माभिः सह प्रीयमाणः (*sajoṣā yuṣmābhiḥ saha prīyamāṇaḥ*): "O gods, I praise the beloved Agni so that he may come with you." Then he explains in another way, यस्तुवर्णिः [?] शीघ्रं यज्ञस्य संभक्ता सजोषा युष्माभिः सह प्रीयमाणस्तमग्निं गृणीषे (*yastuvarṇiḥ [?] śīghraṃ yajñasya saṃbhaktā sajoṣā yuṣmābhiḥ saha prīyamāṇastamagniṃ gṛṇīṣe*): "O gods, I praise that Agni whom you love and who comes quickly to the sacrificial ground." (The supplied text has "ಯಸ್ತುವರ್ಣಿಃ" for the bhāṣya's *yas turvaṇiḥ*; marked [?].)

वरुणः सुकीर्ति: (*varuṇaḥ sukīrti:*) — "in the way in which Varuṇa, being of good fame, has been prayed to make fame for us, in that way": this is Sāyaṇa's explanation. On this matter he has quoted the ṛk

अति यो मंद्रो यजथाय देवः सुकीर्तिं भिक्षे वरुणस्य भूरेः । (*ati yo maṃdro yajathāya devaḥ sukīrtiṃ bhikṣe varuṇasya bhūreḥ |*)
(म. सं २-२८-१) (*(ma. saṃ 2-28-1)*)

It is plain that in this ṛk fame is greatly desired from Varuṇa and praise is offered.

### Printed p. 134

*Running head: [134 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 186].*

अरिगूर्तः (*arigūrtaḥ*) — this word occurs in the Ṛgveda only in this ṛk, nowhere else. Sāyaṇa explains it as अरिगूर्तोऽरिष्पदातृषु [?] सदा शब्दितः (*arigūrto 'riṣpadātṛṣu [?] sadā śabditaḥ*) ["ಅರಿಷ್ಪದಾತೃಷು" as supplied, marked [?]] अरीणां हननायोद्यु- (*arīṇāṃ hananāyodyu-*) क्तो वा (*kto vā*): "one who always makes a sound, that is, reproaches, towards the misers who give nothing to anyone"; or, another meaning is possible: "one ready to destroy enemies".

सूरिः (*sūriḥ*) — the word *sūri* is read among the names of the praiser ((नि. ३-१९) (*(ni. 3-19)*)), meaning "one who praises". And Yāska, having quoted the ṛk

इहेह जाता समवावशीतामरेपसा तन्वा ३ नामभिः स्वैः । (*iheha jātā samavāvaśītāmarepasā tanvā 3 nāmabhiḥ svaiḥ |*)
जिष्णुर्वामन्यः सुमखस्य सूरिर्दिवो अन्यः सुभगः पुत्र ऊहे ii (*jiṣṇurvāmanyaḥ sumakhasya sūrirdivo anyaḥ subhagaḥ putra ūhe ii*)
(ऋ. सं. १-१८१-४) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-181-4)*)

has explained the word *sūri* in it as *īrayitā*, "inciter" ((नि. १२-३) (*(ni. 12-3)*)). The bhāṣyakāra too has taken this meaning in the present ṛk and explained सूरिः ऎंदरॆ प्रेरयिता अयं वरुणः (*sūriḥ èṃdarè prerayitā ayaṃ varuṇaḥ*) as "the inciter Varuṇa".

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

प्रेष्ठम् (*preṣṭham*) ("most dear"): by अतिशायने तमबिष्ठनौ (*atiśāyane tamabiṣṭhanau*) the suffix *iṣṭhan*; by प्रियस्थिर स्थिर—(पा. सू. ६-४-१५७) (*priyasthira sthira—(pā. sū. 6-4-157)*) the substitute *pra* for *priya*; by प्रकृत्यैकाच् (पा. सू. ६-३-१६३) (*prakṛtyaikāc (pā. sū. 6-3-163)*) the natural form remains, so the loss of *ṭi* does not occur; guṇa; because the suffix is marked *n* the first syllable is acute (printed "ಆದ್ಯುದಾತ್ತಸ್ವತರ"). अतिथिम् (*atithim*) (root अत सातत्यगमने (*ata sātatyagamane*)): by ऋतन्यंजि—(उ. सू. ४-४४२) (*ṛtanyaṃji—(u. sū. 4-442)*) the suffix *ithin*; by इत्यादिर्नित्यम् (*ityādirnityam*) the first syllable is acute. गृणीषे (*gṛṇīṣe*) (root ग्य शब्दे (*gya śabde*), *kryādi*): by व्यत्ययो बहुलम् (*vyatyayo bahulam*) the second-person singular ending in place of the first-person singular of the present; by क्रादिभ्यः श्ना (*krādibhyaḥ śnā*) the *śnā* suffix; by स्वादीनां ह्रस्वः (पा. सू. ७-३-८०) (*svādīnāṃ hrasvaḥ (pā. sū. 7-3-80)*) shortening of the root; by ई हल्यघोः (पा. सू. (*ī halyaghoḥ (pā. sū.*) ६-४-११३) (*6-4-113)*) *ī* for the *ā* of the suffix; the change to *ṇ* after *ṛ*; the verb-*nighāta* accent. तुर्वणिः (*turvaṇiḥ*) (root तुर्वी हिंसायां (*turvī hiṃsāyāṃ*)): the Uṇādi suffix *ani*; the last syllable of the suffix is acute. असत् (*asat*) (root अस भुवि (*asa bhuvi*)): *leṭ*; the third person singular *tip*; by इतश्चलोपः (*itaścalopaḥ*) loss of the final *i*; by लेटोऽडाट् (पा. सू. ३-४-६३) (*leṭo 'ḍāṭ (pā. sū. 3-4-63)*) the augment *aḍāṭ* [the sentence ends at the page break; it continues on p. 135 with "ಗಮ"]

### Printed p. 135

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 4 ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 135 (as supplied).*

...the augment *aḍāṭ*. Because it stands at the beginning of the pāda there is no *nighāta* accent; because the augment is unaccented, the accent of the root remains. सुकीर्तिः (*sukīrtiḥ*) ("he whose fame is good"): by नञ्सुभ्याम् (पा. सू. ६-२-१७२) (*nañsubhyām (pā. sū. 6-2-172)*) the last syllable of the later member is acute. पर्षत् (*parṣat*) (root पृ पालनपूरणयोः (*pṛ pālanapūraṇayoḥ*)): *leṭ*; third person singular *śip* [as printed]; loss of *i*; augment *aḍ*; by सिब्बहुलं लेटि (पा. सू. ३-१-३४) (*sibbahulaṃ leṭi (pā. sū. 3-1-34)*) *sip*; on that account guṇa of the *ṛ* of the root with *r*; because of the *r* the *s* of *sip* becomes *ṣ*; the verb-*nighāta* accent.

> धेनुर्यथा दोहाय दोगुर्गृहवागच्छति तद्वत् । किं कुर्वन् । सस्मिन् समान
