#### Ṛk 1

*[The supplied text prints the stray word बॆंगळूरु (*bèṃgaḷūru*) before the Saṃhitā, and the label of the Saṃhitā is missing; the standard label was inserted.]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> पि॒तुं नु स्तो॑षं म॒हो ध॒र्माणं॒ तवि॑षीं ।
> यस्य॑ त्रि॒तो व्योज॑सा वृ॒त्रं विप॑र्वम॒र्दय॑त् ॥ १ ॥

> *pituṃ nu stoṣaṃ maho dharmāṇaṃ taviṣīṃ |*
> *yasya trito vyojasā vṛtraṃ viparvamardayat ‖ 1 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads पदहारः (*padahāraḥ*); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> पितुं । नु । स्तोषं । महः । धर्माणं । तविषीं ।
> यस्य । त्रितः । वि । ओजसा । वृत्रं । विपर्वं । अर्दयत् ॥ १ ॥

> *pituṃ | nu | stoṣaṃ | mahaḥ | dharmāṇaṃ | taviṣīṃ |*
> *yasya | tritaḥ | vi | ojasā | vṛtraṃ | viparvaṃ | ardayat ‖ 1 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads चायुधभाष्यं (*cāyudhabhāṣyaṃ*); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अहमगस्यो नु क्षिप्रं पितुं पालकमन्नं स्तोषं। सौमि। महो महांतं
> धर्माणं सर्वस्य धारकं तविषीं बलात्मकं । यद्वा । महतो लोकस्य धारकं ।
> यस्यान्न स्यौजसा बलेन सामर्थ्येन त्रितो विस्तीर्णतमः प्रख्यातकीर्तिस्त्रिषु क्षित्यादि-
> स्थानेषु तायमानोऽपींद्रो वृत्रं विपर्वं विच्छिन्नसंधिकं यथा तथार्दयत्
> हिंसितवान् ॥

*Gist:* I, Agastya, quickly praise the protecting food, great, the upholder of all, strong, by whose might the famous Trita-Indra tore Vṛtra limb from limb.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads । प्रतिसदर्थ । (*| pratisadartha |*); standard label inserted.]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

यस्य (*yasya*) — of which food. ओजसा (*ojasā*) — by the life-giving power. त्रितः (*tritaḥ*) — Indra, famed in the three worlds (*trita*). वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*) — the demon Vṛtra. विपर्वं (*viparvaṃ*) — (making him) one with severed joints. वि आर्दयत् (*vi ārdayat*) — utterly destroyed. (Such food:) महः (*mahaḥ*) — great. धराणं (*dharāṇaṃ*) — all-supporting (or: of the great world, the upholder). तविषीं (*taviṣīṃ*) — strong. पितुं (*pituṃ*) — protecting food. नु (*nu*) — quickly. स्तोषं (*stoṣaṃ*) — I praise.

### Printed p. 187

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 6] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 187 (as supplied, "ಅ ೨").*

*[The Pratipadārtha, begun on p. 186, ends on p. 187 (from the head महः (*mahaḥ*) on); it is given in full above.]*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads भवार्थ (*bhavārtha*); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

I quickly praise that food which is life-giving, great, the upholder of the world, strong and protecting, by whose life-giving power Indra, famed in all three worlds, cut the joints of the demon Vṛtra and destroyed him.

**Printed English translation**

I glorify Pitu, the great, the upholder, the strong, by whose invigorating power Trita slew the mutilated Vritra.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

पितुं—पालकमन्नं (*pituṃ—pālakamannaṃ*) — "the protecting food": the meaning is food that protects us from the affliction of hunger. The word *pitu* is read among the twenty-eight names of food, *andhaḥ*, *vājaḥ* and others ((नि. ३-९) (*(ni. 3-9)*)). Besides, Yāska:

पितुरित्यन्ननाम पातेर्वा, पिबतेर्वा, प्यायतेर्वा । (*piturityannanāma pātervā, pibatervā, pyāyatervā |*)
(सि. ९-२४) (*(si. 9-24)*)
ऎंदु पितु शब्दद निर्वचनवन्नु हेळिद्दारॆ. ऎंदरॆ—पातेर्वा—नम्मन्नु हसिविनिंद कापाडु (*èṃdu pitu śabdada nirvacanavannu heḷiddārè. èṃdarè—pātervā—nammannu hasiviniṃda kāpāḍu*)

has given the derivation of the word *pitu*: that is, *pātervā*: because it protects us from hunger, *pitu* means food; or because it is used for drinking it is called *pitu*, it also applies to liquid food (milk and the like), so it is used for drinking and may be formed from the root *pā* (*pib*) "to drink"; or it may be formed from the root *pyāy* (the supplied text has "ಪ್ರ್ಯಾಯ" for *pyāyate*), which gives the sense "to satisfy". Taking the very ṛk in which this word occurs as the example, he has given its explanation:

पितुं नु स्तोषं महो धर्माणं तविषीं । (*pituṃ nu stoṣaṃ maho dharmāṇaṃ taviṣīṃ |*)
यस्य त्रितो व्योजसा वृत्रं विपर्वमर्दयत् ॥ (*yasya trito vyojasā vṛtraṃ viparvamardayat ‖*)
तं पितुं स्तौमि महतो धारयितारं बलस्य । तविषीति बलनाम तवतेर्वा (*taṃ pituṃ staumi mahato dhārayitāraṃ balasya | taviṣīti balanāma tavatervā*)
वृद्धिकर्मणः । यस्य त्रित ओजसा बलेन त्रितस्त्रिस्थान इंद्रो वृत्रं विपर्वाणं (*vṛddhikarmaṇaḥ | yasya trita ojasā balena tritastristhāna iṃdro vṛtraṃ viparvāṇaṃ*)
व्यर्दयति ॥ (*vyardayati ‖*)

[the window ends here with the Nirukta passage and its reference (printed "(ನಿ. ೯-೨೫)")].

### Printed p. 188

*Running head: [188 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. Ā. 24. Sū. 187] (the print has "ಆ." for the second abbreviation, as supplied). The window continues the Special Topics of Ṛk 1 of Sūkta 187 (the word *pitu*) from p. 187, after the Nirukta passage.*

... having said this: "I praise the food that gives great strength; Indra, pervading the three worlds, having obtained the nourishment of this food and with its help become mighty, crushed and destroyed all the joints of the demon Vṛtra": this is the sense. The word *pitu* (in the sense of food) occurs in the ṛks ऋ. सं. १-६१-७, १-१३२-६, १-१८७-१, ६-२०-४ (*ṛ. saṃ. 1-61-7, 1-132-6, 1-187-1, 6-20-4*) and others, and in places such as अथ. वे. ४-६-३ ; तै. सं. ५-७-२-४, वा सं २-२०, १२-६५, ऐ. ब्रा. १-१३ (*atha. ve. 4-6-3 ; tai. saṃ. 5-7-2-4, vā saṃ 2-20, 12-65, ai. brā. 1-13*) (the supplied text has "ಹೆಚ್ಚಾಚ" [?] for "ಹೆಚ್ಚಾದ" in the first line; marked [?]).

महो धर्माणं-महांतं धर्माणं सर्वस्य धारकं । महतो लोकस्य (*maho dharmāṇaṃ-mahāṃtaṃ dharmāṇaṃ sarvasya dhārakaṃ | mahato lokasya*) धारकं (*dhārakaṃ*) — "great, the upholder of all; the upholder of the great world": Sāyaṇa's explanation is "that which sustains the whole, that is, the great world and the beings of the world". Yāska says महतो धारयितारं बलस्य (*mahato dhārayitāraṃ balasya*), "that which produces great strength". Since this is an epithet of the word *pituṃ*, and since food has the power to give nourishment and strength to one who partakes of it, both senses fit.

तविषीं—तविषीति बलनाम (*taviṣīṃ—taviṣīti balanāma*) — "*taviṣī* is a name of strength". Since the word *taviṣī* is read among the twenty-eight names of strength, *ojaḥ*, *pājaḥ* and others ((नि. ३-९) (*(ni. 3-9)*)), *taviṣīṃ* means strength, power. It is known to all that men and other animals get strength and power by taking food. Or the Nirukta-authors have given for the word *taviṣī* the derivation तवतेर्वा वृद्धिकर्मणः (*tavatervā vṛddhikarmaṇaḥ*), so that this word is formed from the root *tavati*, "to make grow", and the sense is explained: by taking food the body grows and prospers.

त्रितः (*tritaḥ*) — the bhāṣyakāra explains the word *tritaḥ* as विस्तीर्णतमः (*vistīrṇatamaḥ*), and the Nirukta-author as त्रितिस्तीर्ण- (*tritistīrṇa-*) तमः (नि ४-६) (*tamaḥ (ni 4-6)*): that is, "pervading all, widely extended". Besides, the Nirukta-author in explaining this ṛk says त्रितस्तिस्थान इंद्रः (*tritastisthāna iṃdraḥ*): "Trita is Indra of three places"; *tristhāna* means earth, atmosphere and heaven: the sense is Indra of unobstructed power in all three worlds. *Tritaḥ* may also mean Trita Āptya. The relation between Trita Āptya and Indra, the meaning of the word *trita*, and other related matters we have explained at length in the Ṛ. Saṃ. part 5, pp. 230-231, in the introduction to part 8, pp. iii-viii, and in part 8, pp. 443-447. In the present ṛk *tritaḥ* means Indra, who is glorious in the threefold places, earth, atmosphere and heaven.

वृत्रं विपर्वमर्दयत् (*vṛtraṃ viparvamardayat*) — among Indra's heroic deeds the slaying of Vṛtra is the most important. This is described:

इंद्रस्य नु वीर्याणि प्र वोचं यानि चकार प्रथमानि वज्रे । (*iṃdrasya nu vīryāṇi pra vocaṃ yāni cakāra prathamāni vajre |*)
(ऋ. सं. १-३२-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 1-32-1)*)

### Printed p. 189

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Ā. 5. Varga 6 ] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 189 (as supplied, "೮. ೨" for "ಅ. ೨").*

in the sūkta ऋ सं १-३२ (*ṛ saṃ 1-32*) that begins thus, and in many other places, in many ways. For this very reason the name *vṛtrahā*, "slayer of Vṛtra", is famous for Indra. That Indra slew Vṛtra by his own power is said in the ṛks ऋ. सं. १-१६५-८ ; ७-२१-६, १०-१३८-६ (*ṛ. saṃ. 1-165-8 ; 7-21-6, 10-138-6*) and others. And there are descriptions that for the battle with Vṛtra the gods increased his prowess (ऋ. सं. १०-११३ ८ (*ṛ. saṃ. 10-113 8*)), or supplied him with strength and power (ऋ. सं. १०-८०-५, ६-२०-२, १०-४८-३, १०-१२०-३ (*ṛ. saṃ. 10-80-5, 6-20-2, 10-48-3, 10-120-3*)), or put the thunderbolt in his hand (ऋ. सं २-२०-८ (*ṛ. saṃ 2-20-8*)). Most of all the Maruts are the ones who urge him and support him (ऋ. सं. ३-३२-४, १०-७३-१ (*ṛ. saṃ. 3-32-4, 10-73-1*), etc.); even when all the other gods fled in fear of Vṛtra (ऋ. सं. ८-८५-७, ४-१८-११, ऐ. ब्रा. ३-२० (*ṛ. saṃ. 8-85-7, 4-18-11, ai. brā. 3-20*)) the Maruts alone helped Indra. But it is also said that the Maruts once abandoned him (ऋ. सं. ८-७-३१ (*ṛ. saṃ. 8-7-31*)); and there are descriptions here and there that Agni, Soma and Viṣṇu helped Indra when he fought with Vṛtra. In the Bhāgavata and other Purāṇas there is a long history of the slaying of the demon Vṛtra.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page, noted briefly)

पितुम् (*pitum*) (root पा पाने (*pā pāne*)): by पः किच्च (उ. सू. १-२०) (*paḥ kicca (u. sū. 1-20)*) the suffix *tu*; because it is marked *k*, *i* for the *ā* of the root; by the suffix accent the last syllable is acute. स्तोषम् (*stoṣam*) (root ष्टुञ् स्तुतौ (*ṣṭuñ stutau*)): Vedic *luṅ*; first person singular; the augment *aṭ* does not occur by the Vedic rule; the verb-*nighāta* accent. धर्माणम् (*dharmāṇam*) (root धृञ् धारणे (*dhṛñ dhāraṇe*)): by अन्येभ्योऽपि दृश्यंते (पा. सू. ३-२-७५) (*anyebhyo 'pi dṛśyaṃte (pā. sū. 3-2-75)*) the suffix *manin*; by *vyatyaya* the first syllable of the suffix is acute. विपर्वम् (*viparvam*) ("him from whom the joint is gone"): by बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या (*bahuvrīhau prakṛtyā*) the accent of the earlier member. अर्दयत् (*ardayat*) (root अर्द हिंसायां (*arda hiṃsāyāṃ*)): *curādi*; *luṅ*; third person singular *tip*; loss of *i*; by सत्यापपाश—(पा. सू. ३-१-२५) (*satyāpapāśa—(pā. sū. 3-1-25)*) the suffix *ṇic* in the own sense; because of the *śap* suffix guṇa of *ṇic*; the substitute *ay*; the augment *aṭ* does not occur by the Vedic rule; because of the connection with *yat* there is no *nighāta*; the accent of *ṇic* remains.

### Printed p. 190

*Running head: [190 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24 Sū. 187].*

#### Ṛk 2

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads संख्याशास्त्र (*saṃkhyāśāstra*); standard label inserted. The Saṃhitā has नवृमहे [?] (*navṛmahe [?]*) against the Pada's *vavṛmahe*; marked [?].]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> स्वादो॑ पितो॒ मधो॑ पितो व॒यं त्वा॑ नवृमहे [?] ।
> अ॒स्माक॑मवि॒ता भ॑व ॥ २ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> स्वादो इति । पितो इति । मधो इति । पितो इति । वयं । त्वा । ववृमहे ।
> अस्माकं । अविता । भव ॥ २ ॥

> *svādo iti | pito iti | madho iti | pito iti | vayaṃ | tvā | vavṛmahe |*
> *asmākaṃ | avitā | bhava ‖ 2 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads साह्यभाष्यं (*sāhyabhāṣyaṃ*) (inside the Pada block); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे स्वादो आस्वादनीय पितो पालक मधो माधुर्योपेत पितो हे
> पानसाधनान्न वयं त्वा त्वां ववृमहे । सेवामहे ॥ अत्र सर्वत्राननुदात्तानां
> पूर्वस्यासामान्यवचनत्वेनानिघातत्वं । अनुदात्तानां तु विशेषवचनत्वान्निघातत्वं ॥
> असकृत्पितुशब्दश्रवणं तस्य प्राशस्त्यज्ञापनार्थं । हे पितो अस्माकमविता तर्पयिता
> भव ॥

*Gist:* O savoury, protecting, sweet food, drink-giving: we serve you; be our satisfier. The repetition of *pito* shows its excellence.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

स्वादो—रुचिकरवू (*svādo—rucikaravū*) (tasty). पितो—पालकवू (*pito—pālakavū*) (protecting). मधो—माधुर्योपेतवू (*madho—mādhuryopetavū*) (sweet). पितो— (*pito—*) पानसाधनवू आद ऎलै अन्नवे (*pānasādhanavū āda èlai annave*) (a means of drink): O food! वयं (*vayaṃ*) — we. त्वा (*tvā*) — you. ववृमहे (*vavṛmahe*) — we serve. अस्माकं (*asmākaṃ*) — to us. अविता (*avitā*) — protector (satisfier). भव (*bhava*) — be.

*[As supplied, the label line of the next section reads बनार्थ (*banārtha*); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O food, worthy of being tasted, protecting, sweet and a means of drink, we serve you. Be our satisfier.

**Printed English translation**

Savoury Pitu ; sweet Pitu ; we worship you : become our protector.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

स्वादो पितो मधो पितो (*svādo pito madho pito*) — *svādu* means tasty, wholesome to eat. That *pito* is addressed here twice shows the extreme regard and esteem the praiser has for food.

अविता-तर्पयिता (*avitā-tarpayitā*) — the bhāṣyakāra's explanation is "one who satisfies". *Avitā* may also be understood as "protector": one who removes the affliction of hunger and protects.

### Printed p. 191

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 6] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 191 (as supplied).*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page for Ṛk 2, noted briefly)

स्वादो (*svādo*): being at the beginning of the pāda, no vocative *nighāta* accent; by आमंत्रितस्य— (*āmaṃtritasya—*) (पा. सू. ६-१-१९८ (*pā. sū. 6-1-198*)) the initial syllable is acute. ववृमहे (*vavṛmahe*) (root वृञ् वरणे (*vṛñ varaṇe*)): *laṭ*, first person plural, suffix *mahiṅ*; by टितआत्मनेपादानां टे— (*ṭitaātmanepādānāṃ ṭe—*) (पा सू. ३-४-७९ (*pā sū. 3-4-79*)) the *ṭi* becomes *e*; by बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*) *śluvikaraṇa* and reduplication of the root; the verb-*nighāta* accent. The supplied text then repeats the final word "it comes" once (dropped). अविता (*avitā*) (root अव रक्षणे (*ava rakṣaṇe*)): suffix *tṛc* in the agent sense; by चितः (*citaḥ*) the last syllable is acute. पितो (*pito*): the vocative form of a different noun; since a vocative also precedes, the rule आमंत्रितं पूर्वमविद्यमानवत् (*āmaṃtritaṃ pūrvamavidyamānavat*) (पा. सू. ८-१-७२ (*pā. sū. 8-1-72*)) would make the earlier one count as non-existent, but by नामंत्रिते समानाधिकरणे सामान्यवचनम् (*nāmaṃtrite samānādhikaraṇe sāmānyavacanam*) (पा. सू. ८-१-७९ (*pā. sū. 8-1-79*)) this does not apply; as the head noun is the qualified word and *svādo* the general qualifier, the earlier general word is not treated as absent, so the vocative *nighāta* accent falls on the qualified word. The same holds for the following qualifiers.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उप॑ नः पित॒वा च॑र शि॒वः शि॒वाभि॑रू॒तिभिः॑ ।
> म॒यो॒भुर॑द्विषे॒ण्यः सखा॑ सु॒शेवो॒ अद्व॑याः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उप । नः । पितो इति । आ । चर । शिवः । शिवाभिः । ऊतिभिः ।
> मयोभुः । अद्विषेण्यः । सखा । सुशेवः । अद्वयाः ॥ ३ ॥

> *upa | naḥ | pito iti | ā | cara | śivaḥ | śivābhiḥ | ūtibhiḥ |*
> *mayobhuḥ | adviṣeṇyaḥ | sakhā | suśevaḥ | advayāḥ ‖ 3 ‖*

*[As supplied, a line reading व्याकरणप्रकिया (*vyākaraṇaprakiyā*) stands inside the Pada block where the label of the bhāṣya belongs; standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे पितो यतस्त्वं शिवो मंगलः अतः शिवाभिर्मंगलयुक्ताभिरूतिभी रक्षणै-
> र्नोऽस्माना चर । आगच्छ । आगत्य च मयोभुः सुखस्य भावयिताद्विषेण्योऽद्वेष्य-

> *he pito yatastvaṃ śivo maṃgalaḥ ataḥ śivābhirmaṃgalayuktābhirūtibhī rakṣaṇai-*
> *rno 'smānā cara | āgaccha | āgatya ca mayobhuḥ sukhasya bhāvayitādviṣeṇyo 'dveṣya-*

### Printed p. 192

*Running head: [192 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1 A. 24. Sū. 187] (as supplied).*

> रसः । प्रियरस इत्यर्थः । सखा सखिवत्प्रियकारी सुशेवोऽत एव सुष्टु सुखकरोऽ-
> द्वया द्वयरहित उक्तगुण एव न तु तद्विपरीतो भवेत्यर्थः । अथवा दृष्टांत-
> वादोऽयं । सुशेवोऽद्वया मनःकर्मविसंवादरहितः सखा यथा मयोभुर-
> द्वेष्यश्च भवति तद्वत्त्वमपि भवेत्यर्थः ॥

*Gist:* O Pito, being auspicious, come to us with auspicious aids, giving delight, not distasteful in flavour, friend-like, well respected, free of duplicity; or: as a sincere friend is.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पितो—पालकवाद ऎलै अन्नवे (*pito—pālakavāda èlai annave*) (O protecting food). शिवः—मंगळात्मकनाद नीनु (*śivaḥ—maṃgaḷātmakanāda nīnu*) (you, auspicious). शिवाभिः—मंगळ करगळाद (*śivābhiḥ—maṃgaḷa karagaḷāda*) (auspicious). ऊतिभिः—रक्षणॆगळॊंदिगॆ (*ūtibhiḥ—rakṣaṇègaḷòṃdigè*) (with aids). नः उप—नम्म समीपक्कॆ (*naḥ upa—namma samīpakkè*) (to us, near). आ च—आगमिसु (*ā ca—āgamisu*) (come). सुशेवः—श्रेष्ठवागि गौरविसल्पडुववनू (*suśevaḥ—śreṣṭhavāgi gauravisalpaḍuvavanū*) (greatly respected); अद्वयाः—मनस्सु मत्तु कर्म इवुगळिगॆ ऐक्यतॆ यन्नु संपादिसिरुववनू (*advayāḥ—manassu mattu karma ivugaḷigè aikyatè yannu saṃpādisiruvavanū*) (one who has brought mind and action into unity); सखा—स्नेहितनू (*sakhā—snehitanū*) (friend); मयोभुः—सुखवन्नुंटुमाडुववनू (*mayobhuḥ—sukhavannuṃṭumāḍuvavanū*) (bringer of happiness); अद्विषेण्यः—द्वेषरहितनू आगु (*adviṣeṇyaḥ—dveṣarahitanū āgu*) (be free of hatred). Alternatively: मयोभुः—संतोषकारकनू (*mayobhuḥ—saṃtoṣakārakanū*) (a giver of delight); अद्विषेण्यः— (*adviṣeṇyaḥ—*) अनिष्टवाद रसरहितनागि (प्रियवाद रसवुळ्ळवनू) (*aniṣṭavāda rasarahitanāgi (priyavāda rasavuḷḷavanū)*) (without unpleasant flavour, i.e. of agreeable flavour); सखा—सुहृत्तिनंतॆ प्रियवन्नुंटुमाडु ववनू (*sakhā—suhṛttinaṃtè priyavannuṃṭumāḍu vavanū*) (doing what is dear like a friend); सुशेवः—(आदुदरिंदले) सेविसलु अर्हनू (*suśevaḥ—(ādudariṃdale) sevisalu arhanū*) (therefore worthy of being served); अद्वयाः—पूज्यनू, ग्राह्यनू आद गुणविल्लदे बेरॆ विधवाद गुणविल्लदवनू (आगु) (*advayāḥ—pūjyanū, grāhyanū āda guṇavillade berè vidhavāda guṇavilladavanū (āgu)*) (without any quality other than the stated worshipful and acceptable one; be so).

*[As supplied, the label line reads भाषार्थ (*bhāṣārtha*); standard label inserted.]*

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O auspicious food, come to us with your auspicious aids; be a giver of delight to the sacrificer, of agreeable flavour free of the unpleasant, doing what is dear like a friend, and therefore worthy of worship and of being served.

**Printed English translation**

Come to us, Pitu, auspicious with auspicious aids ; a source of delight not unpalatable, a friend well respected, and having none (bnt [sic] agreeable properties).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

शिवः, शिवाभिः, सुशेवः (*śivaḥ, śivābhiḥ, suśevaḥ*) — the word *śivaḥ* is stated among the twenty names of happiness (नि. ३-१३ (*ni. 3-13*)), so *śivaḥ* means happiness. The bhāṣyakāra explains it as "auspicious". Yāska, in the explanation of the ṛk

अमीवहा वास्तोष्पते विश्वा रूपाण्याविशन् । सखा सुशेव एधि नः । (*amīvahā vāstoṣpate viśvā rūpāṇyāviśan | sakhā suśeva edhi naḥ |*)
(म. सं. ७-५५-१) (*(ma. saṃ. 7-55-1)*)

explains the word *śeva* as follows: शेव इति सुखनाम (*śeva iti sukhanāma*), "*śeva* is a name of happiness", शिष्यतेर्नकारो नामकरणोऽन्तस्थांतरोपलिंगी (*śiṣyaternakāro nāmakaraṇo 'ntasthāṃtaropaliṃgī*) and विभाषितगुण शिवमित्यस्य भवति (*vibhāṣitaguṇa śivamityasya bhavati*) (a grammatical-etymological remark which the print leaves in this form; not explained further here).

### Printed p. 193

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 6] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 193 (as supplied).*

(नि. १०-१७) (*(ni. 10-17)*): having stated the sense "happiness" for the word *śeva*, he shows that *śiva* has the same sense. In lexicons the word *śiva* is given the sense "auspicious". There is no objection to saying that whatever produces happiness is auspicious. Hence in the present ṛk both senses, "producing happiness" and "auspicious", are suitable.

मयोभुः (*mayobhuḥ*) — the word *mayaḥ* too, being stated among the twenty names of happiness beginning with शिंबाता, शतरा [?] (*śiṃbātā, śatarā [?]*) (readings uncertain), means happiness. One who produces happiness is named *mayobhuḥ*. The word *mayobhuḥ* in the sense "producing happiness" is used often in the Ṛgveda and in the other Vedas.

अद्विषेण्यः—अद्वेष्यरसः (*adviṣeṇyaḥ—adveṣyarasaḥ*) — "of a flavour not hateful"; that is, not causing hatred, or having a beneficial flavour (taste). The sense is: dear and tasty.

सखा-सखिवप्रियकारी (*sakhā-sakhivapriyakārī*) — "doing what is dear, like a friend". Since a friend always desires one's good and does what is dear, food too is desirable to all and does good; so the sage addresses food, out of great affection, with the respectful word *sakhā*, "friend".

अद्वयाः—द्वयरहितः उक्तगुण एव न तु तद्विपरीतो भवेत्यर्थः (*advayāḥ—dvayarahitaḥ uktaguṇa eva na tu tadviparīto bhavetyarthaḥ*) — the bhāṣyakāra explains in one way: without any quality other than the worshipful and acceptable one; and in another way, अद्वयाः मनःकर्मविसंवादरहितः (*advayāḥ manaḥkarmavisaṃvādarahitaḥ*): free from disagreement between mind and action, that is, doing good wholeheartedly, without being of one kind in mind and another in deed. In worldly dealings it is not impossible to see those who pretend to be friends speak good words with the mouth only and in action do the contrary, harm. Therefore food is praised as having the nature of doing good while remaining of one kind in both mind and action.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page for Ṛk 3, noted briefly)

चर (*cara*) (root चर गतिभक्षणयोः (*cara gatibhakṣaṇayoḥ*)): *loṭ*, second person singular; the verb-*nighāta* accent. शिवः (*śivaḥ*) (शीते अस्मिन् सर्वमिति शिवः (*śīte asmin sarvamiti śivaḥ*), root शीज् स्वप्ने (*śīj svapne*)): formed by सर्व निघृष्टरिष्टलष्टशिव— (*sarva nighṛṣṭariṣṭalaṣṭaśiva—*) (उ. सू. १-१५१ (*u. sū. 1-151*)) with the suffix *va*, irregularly. अद्विषेण्यः (*adviṣeṇyaḥ*) (root द्विष अप्रीत् (*dviṣa aprīt*)): the Uṇādi suffix *eṇya*; *nañ* as first member; in a bahuvrīhi, by नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) (पा. सू. ६-२-१७२ (*pā. sū. 6-2-172*)) the last syllable of the final member is acute.

*[The supplied text ends with the stray line [13] Vol. 14 (*[13] Vol. 14*); not part of the text.]*

### Printed p. 194

*Running head: [194 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 24. Sū. 187] (as supplied).*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (continuation of the Ṛk 3 grammar page, noted briefly)

सुशेवः (*suśevaḥ*) (root शीज् स्वप्ने (*śīj svapne*)): by इण् शीज्भ्यां वन् (*iṇ śījbhyāṃ van*) (उ. सू. १-१५० (*u. sū. 1-150*)) the suffix *van*; by सार्वधातुकार्ध— (*sārvadhātukārdha—*) guṇa; being marked *n*, the initial syllable is acute. In the compound, overriding the *su* accent, by आद्युदात्तं द्वैच् छंदसि (*ādyudāttaṃ dvaic chaṃdasi*) (पा. सू. ६-२-११९ (*pā. sū. 6-2-119*)) the final member gets the acute on its first syllable. अद्वयाः (*advayāḥ*): by तत्पुरुषे तुल्या— (*tatpuruṣe tulyā—*) (पा. सू. ६-२-२ (*pā. sū. 6-2-2*)) the first member *nañ* keeps its natural accent.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तव॒ तै [?] पि॑तो॒ रसा॒ रजां॒स्यनु॒ विष्ठि॑ताः ।
> दि॒वि वाता॑ इव श्रि॒ताः ॥ ४ ॥

> *tava tai [?] pito rasā rajāṃsyanu viṣṭhitāḥ |*
> *divi vātā iva śritāḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

*[The Saṃhitā has तै [?] (*tai [?]*) against the Pada's *tye*; marked [?].]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तव । त्ये । पितो इति । रसाः । रजांसि । अनु । विस्थिताः ।
> दिवि । वातार्षइव [?] । श्रिताः ॥ ४ ॥

> *tava | tye | pito iti | rasāḥ | rajāṃsi | anu | visthitāḥ |*
> *divi | vātārṣaiva [?] | śritāḥ ‖ 4 ‖*

*[The Pada prints वातार्षइव [?] (*vātārṣaiva [?]*) for *vātāḥ iva*; marked [?].]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे पितो तव त्वत्संबंधिनस्ये ते स्वाद्वम्लादिषड्रसा रजांसि लोकाल्लोक-
> स्थानार्थानन्वानुकूल्येन विष्ठिताः । विविधं स्थिताः । विविधस्थितौ दृष्टांतः । दिवि द्युलो-
> केंतरिक्षे श्रिता वाता नायवः ते यथा व्याप्तस्तद्वत् ॥

*Gist:* O Pito, those six flavours of yours, sweet, sour and the rest, are variously spread through the worlds as the winds are spread in the sky.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पितो—ऎलै अन्नवे (*pito—èlai annave*) (O food). दिवि—अंतरिक्षदल्लि (*divi—aṃtarikṣadalli*) (in the atmosphere). श्रिताः—आश्रयिसि प्रसरिसिरुव (*śritāḥ—āśrayisi prasarisiruva*) (spread, resting). वाताः इव—वायुगळु सर्वत्र व्याप्तवागिरुवंतॆ (*vātāḥ iva—vāyugaḷu sarvatra vyāptavāgiruvaṃtè*) (as the winds are diffused everywhere). ते--निन्न (*te--ninna*) (your). त्ये रसाः—प्रसिद्धवाद आम्लादि षड्र सगळू सह (*tye rasāḥ—prasiddhavāda āmlādi ṣaḍra sagaḷū saha*) (the well-known six flavours, sour and the rest). रजांसि—लोकगळन्नॆल्ला (*rajāṃsi—lokagaḷannèllā*) (all the worlds). अनुविष्ठिताः—लोकक्कॆ अनुकूलकरवागिरुवंतॆ विध विधवागि प्रसरिसि व्यापिसिवॆ (*anuviṣṭhitāḥ—lokakkè anukūlakaravāgiruvaṃtè vidha vidhavāgi prasarisi vyāpisivè*) (are spread in many ways, favourably for the world).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O food, as the winds spread in the atmosphere pervade everywhere, so your six flavours, sour and the rest, pervade all the worlds in many ways and are favourable to the world.

### Printed p. 195

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 6] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 195 (as supplied).*

**Printed English translation**

Your flavours, Pitu, are diffused through the regions, as the winds are spread through the sky.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

त्ये—ते (*tye—te*) — wherever the word *tya* occurs in the Ṛgveda it has the sense of the word *tad*, "that". The Nirukta-authors

त्यमू षु वाजिनं देवजूतं सहावानं तरुतारं रथानां । (*tyamū ṣu vājinaṃ devajūtaṃ sahāvānaṃ tarutāraṃ rathānāṃ |*)
(ऋ. सं. १०-१७८-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 10-178-1)*)

प्रति त्यं चारुमध्वरं गोपीथाय प्र हूयसे । मरुद्धिरग्न आ गहि ॥ (*prati tyaṃ cārumadhvaraṃ gopīthāya pra hūyase | maruddhiragna ā gahi ‖*)
(ऋ. सं १-१९-१) (*(ṛ. saṃ 1-19-1)*)

in these and other ṛks explain the word *tyaṃ* as *taṃ*, "him" (नि. १०-२९, ३७ (*ni. 10-29, 37*)).

रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*) — the word *rajaḥ* is read among the thirty names of night, *śyāvī*, *kṣapā* and others (नि. २-१८ (*ni. 2-18*)), and Yāska, explaining the sense of *rajaḥ*, says:

रजः । रजो रजतेर्जोर्यती रज उच्यते । उदकं रज उच्यते । लोका (*rajaḥ | rajo rajaterjoryatī raja ucyate | udakaṃ raja ucyate | lokā*)
रजांस्युच्यंते ॥ (*rajāṃsyucyaṃte ‖*)
(नि. ४-१९) (*(ni. 4-19)*)

Since it is derived from the root *rajati* in the sense "to shine", *rajaḥ* means light; *rajaḥ* also means water; the plural *rajāṃsi* also means worlds: so Yāska explains, and as an example

भुवो यज्ञस्य रजसश्च नेता यत्रा नियुद्भिः सचसे शिवाभिः । (*bhuvo yajñasya rajasaśca netā yatrā niyudbhiḥ sacase śivābhiḥ |*)
दिवि मूर्धानं दधिषे स्वर्षां जिह्वामग्ने चकृषे हव्यवाहं ॥ (*divi mūrdhānaṃ dadhiṣe svarṣāṃ jihvāmagne cakṛṣe havyavāhaṃ ‖*)
(ऋ. सं. १०-८-६) (*(ṛ. saṃ. 10-8-6)*)

shows that in this ṛk the word *rajasaḥ* has the sense "water", and in

रजांसि चित्रा विचरंति तान्यवः । (*rajāṃsi citrā vicaraṃti tānyavaḥ |*)
अद्या चिन्नू चित्रदपो नदीनां यदाभ्यो अरदो गातुमिंद्र । (*adyā cinnū citradapo nadīnāṃ yadābhyo arado gātumiṃdra |*)
नि पर्वता अद्मसदो न सेदुस्त्वया दृळ्हानि सुकृतो रजांसि ॥ (*ni parvatā admasado na sedustvayā dṛḷhāni sukṛto rajāṃsi ‖*)
ऋ. सं. (६-३०-३) (*ṛ. saṃ. (6-30-3)*)

and other ṛks that the word *rajāṃsi* has the sense "worlds". In the present ṛk the sense is "worlds".

रसाः दिवि वाता इव श्रिताः (*rasāḥ divi vātā iva śritāḥ*) — just as the wind in the atmosphere spreads evenly everywhere, so the six flavours, sweet and the rest, pervade food and produce flavours of many kinds: the sage here gives this as an illustration.

### Printed p. 196

*Running head: [196 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. ಗ. 24. Sū. 187] (as supplied, the print has "ಗ." for the first abbreviation).*

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page for Ṛk 4, noted briefly)

रजांसि (*rajāṃsi*) (root रंज रागे (*raṃja rāge*)): by भूरंजिभ्यां कित् (*bhūraṃjibhyāṃ kit*) (उ. सू. ६-५६ (*u. sū. 6-56*)) the suffix *asun*; because it is made *kit*, by अनिदितां हल उपधा (*aniditāṃ hala upadhā*) the nasal is dropped; the suffix being marked *n*, the first syllable is acute. विष्ठिताः (*viṣṭhitāḥ*) (root ष्ठा गतिनिवृत्तौ (*ṣṭhā gatinivṛttau*)): by निष्ठा (*niṣṭhā*) the suffix *kta*; by द्यतिस्यति मास्था— (*dyatisyati māsthā—*) (पा. सू ७-४.४० (*pā. sū 7-4.40*)) the *ā* of the root becomes *i*; by गतिरनंतरः (*gatiranaṃtaraḥ*) (पा. सू. ६-१-४९ (*pā. sū. 6-1-49*)) the preverb (*vi*) takes the *nighāta* accent. Two stray marks of the supplied text follow ("4" and a ruled line with ":0:"); not part of the text.

#### Ṛk 5

*[As supplied, the label line reads संभाषणॆ (*saṃbhāṣaṇè*); standard label inserted. The Saṃhitā again has तै [?] (*tai [?]*) against the Pada's *tye*; marked [?].]*

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> तव॒ तै [?] पि॑तो॒ दद॑त॒स्तव॑ स्वादिष्ट॒ ते पि॑तो ।
> प्र स्वा॒द्मानो॒ रसा॑नां तुवि॒ग्रीवा॑ इवेरते ॥ ५ ॥

> *tava tai [?] pito dadatastava svādiṣṭa te pito |*
> *pra svādmāno rasānāṃ tuvigrīvā iverate ‖ 5 ‖*

*[As supplied, the label line of the Pada reads । पदराणि॥ (*| padarāṇi‖*); standard label inserted.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> तव । त्ये । पितो इति । ददतः । तव । स्वादिष्ट । ते । पितो इति ।
> प्र । स्वाद्मानः । रसानां । तुविग्रीवाः इव । ईरते ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे पितो पालकान्न तै ते त्वदर्थिनो नरास्तव भोक्तारो भवंतीति शेषः ।
> के ते नराः । हे स्वादिष्ट स्वादुतम पितो पालक तव त्वदनुग्रहात्ते त्वां ददतः
> प्रयच्छंतो भवंति । यद्वा । ते पितो इत्यादरार्थं । अभ्यासे हि भूयांसमर्थं
> मन्यंते । किंच तव रसानां स्वाद्वम्लादीनां षण्णां स्वाद्मानः स्वादयितारो भक्ष-
> यितार एव तुविग्रीवा इव । तुवीति बहुनाम । प्रवृद्धग्रीवा एव प्रेरते । प्रकर्षण
> गच्छंति । संचरंति । इवशब्द विवकारार्थः पूरणो वा । भूलोकेत्तार एव
> दृढांगा भवंतीति प्रसिद्धं । अनशने हि ग्रीवा अधो लंबंते । यद्वा । तव रसाना-
> मत्तारोऽसंख्यातग्रीवा इव भवंति रसानां बाहुल्यादिति भावः । एवं बहुरसो-
> पेतमन्नमिति तस्य स्तुतिः ॥

*Gist:* O Pito, men who seek you become your eaters and givers by your favour; the eaters of your flavours go out like men with swelling necks, since fasting makes the neck hang down.

### Printed p. 197

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 6] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 197 (as supplied).*

**Pratipadārtha** (Ṛk 5)

स्वादिष्ट—अत्यंत स्वादुकरनाद (*svādiṣṭa—atyaṃta svādukaranāda*) (most sweet). पितो—पालकनाद अन्नवे,  (*pito—pālakanāda annave, *)(protecting food). त्ये—निन्नन्नु अन्वेषण माडतक्क नररु (*tye—ninnannu anveṣaṇa māḍatakka nararu*) (the men who seek you). तव—निन्नन्नु सेविसि सुखपडुत्तारॆ. (*tava—ninnannu sevisi sukhapaḍuttārè.*) (they enjoy by partaking of you). पितो—रक्षकनाद अन्नवे (*pito—rakṣakanāda annave*) (O protecting food). तव--निन्न अनुग्रहदिंद (*tava--ninna anugrahadiṃda*) (by your favour). ते—अवरु (यज्ञकर्तरु) (*te—avaru (yajñakartaru)*) (they, the sacrificers). ददतः—निन्नन्नु (इतररिगू) हंचुत्तारॆ (*dadataḥ—ninnannu (itararigū) haṃcuttārè*) (they distribute you, to others also). रसानां- निन्न षड्रसगळ (*rasānāṃ- ninna ṣaḍrasagaḷa*) (of your six flavours). स्वाद्मानः—भोक्तृगळु (निन्न रसास्वादनॆयन्नु माडि सुखपडतक्कवरु ) (*svādmānaḥ—bhoktṛgaḷu (ninna rasāsvādanèyannu māḍi sukhapaḍatakkavaru )*) (enjoyers, those who are happy relishing your flavours). तुविग्रीवा इव—वृद्धियन्नु हॊंदि पुष्टवाद कत्तुळ्ळवरंतॆ (*tuvigrīvā iva—vṛddhiyannu hòṃdi puṣṭavāda kattuḷḷavaraṃtè*) (like those grown and with stout necks). प्र ईरते—आनंदवन्नु व्यक्तपडि सुत्ता संचरिसुत्तारॆ (*pra īrate—ānaṃdavannu vyaktapaḍi suttā saṃcarisuttārè*) (they move about expressing joy).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O most sweet, protecting food: the sacrificers who partake of you find happiness in you, and not only that, they share that happiness with others. All who relish your six flavours and enjoy happiness, grown and with stout necks as it were, move about expressing joy.

**Printed English translation**

Those (men), Pitu, (are the enjoyers of your bounty), who are your distributors, most sweet Pitu, (to others): they who are relishers, of your flavours, are as if they had stiff necks.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

*[In the Special Topics of this Ṛk the supplied text has तै [?] (*tai [?]*) (apparently *te*) in the line on *svādmānaḥ rasānām*; marked [?].]*

त्ये—ते (*tye—te*) — we explained the sense of this word under the previous ṛk. Here *te* means men (or animals?) who desire food and partake of it; this is the intended sense.

तव (*tava*) — since the word *tava* is read twice in this ṛk, according to the Nirukta-authors' statement अभ्यासे हि भूयांसमर्थं मन्यंते (*abhyāse hi bhūyāṃsamarthaṃ manyaṃte*) ("in repetition they take the sense to be greater"), the repetition indicates respect and affection for food.

ददतः तव (*dadataḥ tava*) — those who give (you) food to others, by your favour, who make gifts; and

स्वाद्मानः रसानां (*svādmānaḥ rasānāṃ*) — those who relish the many kinds of flavours in food, or who eat food of this kind: both are qualifiers of the word तै [?] (नराः) (*tai [?] (narāḥ)*), "men". The main sense is: men who eat food endowed with the six flavours themselves and also give it to others (become strong).

### Printed p. 198

*Running head: [198 Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya-sahitā [Maṃ. 1. Ā. 24. Sū. 187] (as supplied).*

तुविग्रीवा इव (*tuvigrīvā iva*) — तुवि (*tuvi*) means "much" (नि ३-१३ (*ni 3-13*)); ग्रीव (*grīva*) means neck, throat. Sāyaṇa has given this word *tuvigrīvā* two kinds of meaning. One: प्रवृद्धग्रीवाः (*pravṛddhagrīvāḥ*), "those with well-grown, stout and firm necks". Having stout necks means being firm-limbed. It is well known in the world that by taking good food not the neck alone but the whole body grows strong. Although only the word *grīva*, "neck", is used here, it indicates the other limbs of the body or the whole body; therefore *tuvigrīvā iva* is explained as "moving about firm-limbed". The bhāṣyakāra has explained that the word *iva* here has the sense of *eva*, or that it has no definite sense and is used only to fill the pāda. And explaining the second meaning of the word *tuvigrīvā iva* he says:

तव रसानामत्तारोऽसंख्यातग्रीवा इव भवंति रसानां बाहुल्यादिति भावः । अन्नद (*tava rasānāmattāro 'saṃkhyātagrīvā iva bhavaṃti rasānāṃ bāhulyāditi bhāvaḥ | annada*)

The one who eats food of many kinds of flavours is like one eating with many throats (mouths), that is, becomes a great eater: this is the other kind of meaning given. In this matter the English scholar Wilson explains Sāyaṇa's view in this way:

तुविग्रीवा इवेरते (*tuvigrīvā iverate*)—तुवि (*tuvi*) is ordinarily, a synonyme of बहु (*bahu*) much, many ; Sayana explains it by प्रवृद्ध (*pravṛddha*), enlarged; because, he says, when people eat, they are दृढांग (*dṛḍhāṃga*), firm ; and when they are without food, the neck is bent, bends down ; or it may mean that they are great eaters, as if they had many throats.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā** (grammar page for Ṛk 5, noted briefly)

ददतः (*dadataḥ*) (root डुदाञ् दाने (*ḍudāñ dāne*)): the participle substituted for *laṭ*, *śluvikaraṇa*, reduplication of the root; by श्नाभ्यस्तयोरातः (*śnābhyastayorātaḥ*) (पा. सू. ६-४-११२ (*pā. sū. 6-4-112*)) the *ā* of the root is dropped; in the nominative plural by नाभ्यस्ताच्छतुः (*nābhyastācchatuḥ*) the augment *num* does not occur; by अभ्यस्तानामादि: (*abhyastānāmādi:*) (पा. सू. ६-१-१८९ (*pā. sū. 6-1-189*)) the first syllable is acute. स्वादिष्ट (*svādiṣṭa*): by अतिशायने तमबिष्ठनौ (*atiśāyane tamabiṣṭhanau*) the superlative sense after *svādu*; by the rule टेः (*ṭeḥ*) the *ṭi* (*u*) is dropped; the vocative takes the vocative *nighāta* accent. स्वाद्मानः (*svādmānaḥ*) (root स्वाद आस्वादने (*svāda āsvādane*)): by अन्येभ्योपिदृश्यंते (*anyebhyopidṛśyaṃte*) (पा. सू. ३-२-७५ (*pā. sū. 3-2-75*)) the suffix *manin*; being marked *n*, the first syllable is acute; nominative plural. ईरते (*īrate*) (root ईर गत् प्रेरणे च (*īra gat preraṇe ca*)): *adādi*, *laṭ*, third person plural; the verb-*nighāta* accent.

### Printed p. 199

*Running head: [Aṣṭaka 2. Adhyāya 5. Varga 7] Ṛgveda-saṃhitā 199 (as supplied).*

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> त्वे पि॑तो म॒हानां॑ दे॒वानां॒ मनो॑ हि॒तं ।
> अका॑रि॒ चारु॑ के॒तुना॒ तवाहि॒मव॑सावधीत् ॥ ६ ॥

> *tve pito mahānāṃ devānāṃ mano hitaṃ |*
> *akāri cāru ketunā tavāhimavasāvadhīt ‖ 6 ‖*

*[As supplied, a line reading हडपाटॆ (*haḍapāṭè*) stands inside the Saṃhitā block where the Pada label belongs; standard label inserted.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> त्वे इति । पितो इति । महानां । देवानां । मनः । हितं ।
> अकारि । चारु । केतुना । तव । अहिं । अवसा । अवधीत् ॥ ६ ॥

> *tve iti | pito iti | mahānāṃ | devānāṃ | manaḥ | hitaṃ |*
> *akāri | cāru | ketunā | tava | ahiṃ | avasā | avadhīt ‖ 6 ‖*

*[As supplied, a line reading स:यणभाष्यं (*sa:yaṇabhāṣyaṃ*) stands inside the Pada block where the bhāṣya label belongs; standard label inserted.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे पितो महानां पूज्यानां महतां ना देवानामिंद्रादीनां मनस्त्वे
> त्वयि हितं निहितमकारि । कृतं । अमृतमपि विहाय त्वय्येव वर्तते । हे पितो तव
> चारु समीचीनेन केतुना त्वत्कृतेन प्रज्ञानलक्षणेनावसा रक्षणेनाहिं मेघं वृत्रं
> नावधीत् । हतवानिंद्रः । अन्नमयं हि सोम्य मनः । छां. ६ ५-४ । इति श्रुतेः ।
> अथवा त्वत्केतुना जगद्रक्षणेन निमित्तेनेति योज्यं । जगद्रक्षणार्थं हींद्रेणाहि-
> र्भिद्यते ॥

*Gist:* The minds of the great gods are set on you, Pito; by your guarding wisdom Indra slew Ahi, for mind is made of food (Chāndogya).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

पितो—ऎलै अन्नवे (*pito—èlai annave*) (O food). महानां—पूज्यराद (*mahānāṃ—pūjyarāda*) (venerable). देवानां—इंद्रादि देवतॆगळ (*devānāṃ—iṃdrādi devatègaḷa*) (of the gods, Indra and others). मनः—मनस्सु (*manaḥ—manassu*) (mind). त्वे—निन्नल्ले (*tve—ninnalle*) (in you alone). हितं अकारि—इडल्पट्टिदॆ (*hitaṃ akāri—iḍalpaṭṭidè*) (has been placed). तव—निन्न (*tava—ninna*) (your). चारु—रम्यवादुदू (*cāru—ramyavādudū*) (pleasant). केतुना—प्रज्ञानरूपवादुदू आद (*ketunā—prajñānarūpavādudū āda*) (of the nature of wisdom). अवसा—रक्षणदिंद (*avasā—rakṣaṇadiṃda*) (by protection). अहिं—मेघवन्नु (अथवा वृत्र नन्नु) (*ahiṃ—meghavannu (athavā vṛtra nannu)*) (the cloud, or Vṛtra). अवधीत्—इंद्रनु कॊंदनु (*avadhīt—iṃdranu kòṃdanu*) (Indra slew).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O food, even the venerable gods, Indra and the rest, have their minds fixed on you alone. By your pleasant protection, of the nature of wisdom, Indra slew Vṛtra.

**Printed English translation**

The thoughts of the mighty gods are fixed. Pitu, upon you, by your kind and intelligent assistance, (Indra) slew Ahi.
